CORRECTS a note carried from proposal 0076 saying tier7a is outside the walk: it is inside `componentAndAboveTierDirs()`. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-test/cmd_validate.go:125-139,:716-793.
- **DECISION: each new spec-map entry lands in the checklist step that CREATES the file it maps** — S9 the tier-3 DIRECTORY entry `tests/tier3_contract/adapter_bind_epoch/...`, S10 the tier-7a and tier-9 file entries, S14 the tier-10 file entry. A trailing registration step (proposal 0073's S22 precedent) leaves tier 0 red on every intermediate commit from S9 to S13, which the checklist's own per-step tier rule forbids; the directory form keeps S10's added tier-3 file covered without a second entry. `tests/tier11_docs` is outside the walk, so the DOCS gates owe no entry.
- **DECISION: DOCS-2 exists, against `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md`, and folds into the EXISTING S6 docs step** with `Depends on:` widened from `S5` to `S2, S5`. A step of its own would renumber S7 through S14 and every `Depends on:` naming them for no separation the docs lane needs. This discharges the standing eleven-lens Deferred.
- **DECISION: DOCS-2 splits into a rewritten one-line `Shutdown` table row plus ONE prose block after `**Scrub responsibilities.**`.** The tier-11 gate reads the row with `lineContaining(page, "| `Shutdown` |")`, so the row must stay one physical line; the epoch contract and the reclaim hold are what §15.4 publishes to third-party authors and belong in prose. EVIDENCE: tests/tier11_docs/basic_level_echo_stamp_doc_reconciliation_test.go:294-311.
- **The `Shutdown` row at `adapter-contract.md:75` is now falsified in FOUR ways, three of them new since the pre-amendment loops read it.** Pre-amendment: the usage flush and runtime close move under `started`, and the `ReportSessionScrub` moves under `live`. New: CODE-1 moves `drainViaLifecycle` INSIDE the `if started {` block, so the drain signal needs `started && !boundRemains` where the row states only "leaves the pod holding no other bound session"; and a mismatched bind epoch makes the adapter perform NEITHER teardown. A DOCS-2 that covers only the two older halves is incomplete.
- **DECISION: `compensateFailedSlotBind` narrows to scalars,** `(ctx, cl, sessionID string, cleanupTimeoutSeconds int, maxConcurrentSessions int32, sandboxName, slotID string) bool`, because `Binder.Resume` holds a `podsession.ResumeRequest` and no `SlotBindRequest` is in scope there; `ResumeRequest` carries all three fields under the same names (binder.go:605,:645,:668). ALTERNATIVES rejected: a synthetic `SlotBindRequest` on the resume path (invents a conversion the proposal never sanctions), a shared `compensationParams` struct (a third parameter-carrying type for three scalars), and dropping `slotID` (the resume branch names a separately reserved slot id). The precedents are CODE-5's `accountSlotFailure` narrowing and CODE-4's own `slotCleanupBudget`. This closes the five-lens standing Open. The narrowed list is stated in exactly TWO prose sites and S12 keeps no signature at all: two prose copies of one signature is the drift that produced the finding.
- **DECISION: the §7.3 resume classification gap is closed by ONE arm, `case status.Code(err) == codes.Aborted: return true` in `isTransientPodClaimError`, owned by CODE-5.** The refusal crosses a process boundary, so only the status code survives: `errSlotReclaimInProgress` and `isSlotReclaimInProgress` are unexported and adapter-local, and no production file under `pkg/gateway` imports `pkg/adapter`. `status.Code` walks the wrap chain (grpc v1.80.0 `status.FromError` falls back to `errors.As` for `GRPCStatus()`), so the arm fires through the `*SlotBindError` CODE-4 makes `Binder.Resume` return. The function already carries one code-reading arm (`SetupCommandFailure`). Test home is `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_setup_demotion_internal_test.go` (`package sessionserver`, `seedResumingRow` fixture at :23, a `holdOrFailOnResumeError` table at :48-110), never `start_test.go`, which is the external package. Only `pkg/adapter/checkpoint.go:115` answers `Aborted` today, so the arm widens nothing unintended.
- **`holdOrFailOnResumeError` and `writePodClaimError` are two different classifiers and only one was broken.** The wire envelope was already right: `writePodClaimError`'s default arm answers a retryable 503 `RESUME_FAILED` for any unrecognised cause (start.go:87,:208-219,:3517). Only the ROW-STATE classifier demoted the row terminal. `SlotBindError.Reason()` is a third classifier on a third path; a fix that touches one must say which.
- **`codes.Aborted` takes `Reason()`'s transient default at EVERY stage.** `Reason()` switches on the code first and only `FailedPrecondition`/`PermissionDenied` refine on `e.Stage`, so the staged start-stage and workspace-stage `Aborted` test rows cover the credential and setup stages too. EVIDENCE: podsession/slotfailure.go:84-101.
- **The credential stage's adapter error is NOT wrapped in `*CredentialAssignmentError`.** `assignSlotCredentials` returns that typed error only for a gateway-side lease-minting failure (`AssignProto`/`MintProto`); the `cl.AssignCredentials` RPC error returns bare and `materializeSlot` wraps it as a plain `*SlotBindError` at the `credential_assignment` stage. So a hold refusal on the credential path does not detour through the typed handling at start.go:89/:3653. EVIDENCE: podsession/slotbinder.go:369-403,:308-313.
- **`ensureSlotStateLocked` has exactly FIVE production resolve sites and the hold refusal reaches exactly the seven bind-sequence RPCs.** `ensureSlotPaths` (staging.go:134,:181,:337), `assignCredentialsSlot` (slotcreds.go:26) and `claimSessionSlotUnderLock` (slotsession.go:75). Every other entry-resolving handler goes through `boundSlotState`/`checkSessionBound`, and `RotateCredentials`/`RevokeCredentials`/`ExtendCredentialLease` through `slotStateLocked`. No sixth site. Re-derived by five lenses.
- **The SDK-warm one-session-only refusal runs BEFORE `ensureSlotStateLocked`** (slotsession.go:65-73), so putting the hold refusal at the top of `ensureSlotStateLocked` cannot reorder that control.
- **`deregisterSlot` (the `s.mu`-taking wrapper CODE-6 retires) has exactly two callers left and both are tests,** at `pkg/adapter/podmcp_arming_internal_test.go:88,:245`, a file already in the proposal's test list. Retiring it costs nothing else.
- **The `f1` firing resolved open decision 12 and deleted it: the reclaim hold ends when the cleanup pass that took it RETURNS, whether that cleanup succeeded or failed.** No staged text was edited, because the staging already states it twice in §5.2 and mirrors it in §15.4, and CODE-6 stages an unconditional `defer release()`. The two terminals that looked like they collided are different objects: `leaked` is the pod's Redis slot-counter occupancy held until pod termination, while the hold is adapter-local over the identifier, so a `leaked` slot keeps its occupancy without keeping its identifier held. Shipped behaviour already discards the tree-removal error outright (`_ = removeSlotTree(st)`).
- **The `f1` firing kept open decision 17 (the create-time-reserved retry's residue) with the human and rewrote it in place** so it is answerable in one sitting: harm first, then a low-confidence recommendation to accept as recorded, the ground, the three closures and why each lost. It is the only entry carrying a recommendation, and the section preamble was corrected to say so. Its "recorded under `## Decisions`" citation was FALSE — no such section exists in any file of this proposal; the record is `## Edge cases and accepted failure modes`.
- **The `f1` firing rewrote the 0080 §1.19 impacts row and the sentence it retired was wrong on BOTH halves.** "No bind-sequence RPC gains a refusal, so the class inventory grows by the reclaim hold's `ABORTED` refusal alone" is false: `errSlotReclaimInProgress` is raised at the top of `ensureSlotStateLocked`, which the bind-sequence and workspace RPCs are exactly what reach. The row now states that the hold adds one more producer of an arm §1.19 already enumerates (`boundSlotState` answers absent and unbound alike with one `codes.FailedPrecondition`, slotsession.go:274-283), that the hold's own refusal sits outside §1.19's inventory, and that the class SET is unchanged while membership moves.
- **Retuning `ceil(maxConcurrentSessions/2)` stays out of scope with NO row in the not-staged defects section,** because the proposal is silent on whether the value is right and a tuning question is not a defect it knowingly leaves. The Non-goals bullet is the record. Do not read the call as an assertion that the threshold is correct.
- **`resolvePrepareStagingDir` returns `(string, error)` and drops everything `ensureSlotPaths` gives it except `paths.Staging`** (staging.go:31,:116,:133-138), so any "epoch on the seven responses" work has to widen that helper too, not only `ensureSlotPaths`. FILED.
- **The compensation is synchronous inside `materializeSlot`,** so the gateway's own §5.2 retry can never race its own attempt's reclaim: `applySlotRetryPolicy` re-enters `BindSlot` only after `materializeSlot` returned. Every racing "retry" in the design and in the tier-4 late-reclaim arm is necessarily a fresh client request or a second replica.
- **`reclaimSlotLocked` inserts a hold only when it removed an entry,** and its `release` is a non-nil idempotent no-op otherwise, which is what stops a concurrent unfenced `Shutdown` (removed=false) from clearing another caller's hold on its deferred release. Subtle and correct as staged; do not "simplify" it.
- **The `budget/2` split's whole chain was verified end to end against the shipped adapter.** `contextWithGraceDeadline(parent, grace)` returns `WithTimeout(parent, grace)` for a positive grace and the parent unchanged for a non-positive one, and `resolveShutdownGrace` prefers that ctx's remaining time over the configured grace and the package default, so `ShutdownReclaim(rctx=budget, deadline=budget/2)` really does give `Runtime.Close` budget/2 and leave budget/2 of RPC deadline for the uninterruptible `removeSlotTree`. EVIDENCE: session.go:327-332; mcpruntime.go:308-323; socketruntime.go:457.
- **The §10.1.4 hold-termination pass cannot leak a reclaim hold, and it can hold several identifiers at once.** `deregisterStartedSessions` (slotsession.go:375-396) builds `members` only from entries it removed, under ONE `s.mu`, so CODE-6's `release func()` must be captured per member inside that loop; `onHoldTimeout` then runs `terminateHeldSession` for every member in a plain loop with no early break under a shared 10s context, and that function holds no `s.mu`, so a `defer release()` there cannot deadlock. The whole set is held from pass 1 until pass 2's last member returns. Nothing in the proposal says the hold set can carry several identifiers; it is correct, but a reader of CODE-6 alone will not expect it.
- **`heldSession` and `deregisterStartedSessions` live in `pkg/adapter/slotsession.go` (:306-313, :375), not `holdstate.go`,** even though CODE-6's prose attributes the latter to holdstate.go. The Files-touched list gets it right; do not spend a verifier pair on the prose.
- **CONF-1 says "Four properties, one per part of the §15.4 contract" while SPEC-5 lands TWO §15.4 blocks,** and all four properties are bind-epoch. The reclaim hold has no CONF-1 property, and neither does §15.4's `PrepareWorkspace` non-conformance clause.
- **`docs/reference/adapter-contract.md` is written for RUNTIME authors, not third-party ADAPTER authors** ("the protocol between the Lenny adapter sidecar and your runtime binary", :10; "Your runtime binary never sees them directly", :53). A finding demanding the full §15.4 bind-epoch and reclaim-hold contract be republished there is over-reach; the defensible scope is the `Shutdown` row's now-false statements.
- **The docs surface this proposal can falsify is small and now fully enumerated.** `grep -rln "Shutdown" docs/` returns three files and only `adapter-contract.md` describes the gateway→adapter RPC; the other two are the runtime-facing JSONL `shutdown` frame. The operator-narrative half is inert: no operator page or runbook enumerates leaked-slot or pod-replacement causes. SPEC-3's two added cleanup actions have no docs mirror. The new §6.2 edge reaches no SVG (`pod-warm-path.svg` and `sdk-warm-path.svg` carry `receiving_uploads` as a POD-level phase and no `slot_cleanup` node at all), so DOCS-1's table row is the whole docs surface for SPEC-4.
- **`spec/06:283`'s `**State storage:**` paragraph says the fine session-lifecycle states are NOT projected onto the CRD,** which is the structural reason no Kubernetes-idiom lens has purchase on the §6.2 fence edit. The Kubernetes lens has now returned zero findings on this proposal in eight consecutive runs.
- **`ClaimSlot` pass 1 filters on the per-pod claim, `claimstate.IsTerminal`, the tenant pin and `expiredByUptime`, with NO `sb.Status.Phase` read; only pass 2 reads the phase.** That is the idiomatically correct answer (do not block a synchronous bind on a level-triggered controller), so `ExcludePods` is right for the right reason. EVIDENCE: podclaim/slotclaimer.go:411-478.
- **`s.podBinder` already satisfies the unexported `slotBinder` interface,** so CODE-5's new `resumeOnPod` caller of `accountSlotFailure(ctx, s.podBinder, …)` compiles without widening it. EVIDENCE: start.go:2725-2737.
- **`RecordFailure` has exactly ONE production call site in the shipped tree, `applySlotRetryPolicy` (start.go:2854),** and CODE-5 adds two more. Any proposal sentence saying "whose only production call site is `applySlotRetryPolicy`" is a pre-amendment fact the amendment falsifies.
- **The §5.2 exclusion ("did not complete") and §7.1's "an attempt that meets the hold is one the retry policy did not place") are bridged by the handler blocking on `removeSlotTree`:** a COMPLETED reclaim's answer arrives only after the cleanup finished, so the hold is released before a retry can be placed. The one interleaving that breaks the bridge needs TWO `Shutdown`s for one session, which the gateway does not send. Under CODE-6 there is a second, narrower producer: an `ABSENT` answer can return while the adapter's OWN pre-`Runtime.Start` rollback still holds the identifier, reachable only when the gateway's context expired mid-handler. Below the bar; the outcome is the accepted transient refusal.
- **DECISION (`f1.open-decisions.15`): §7.1's exclusive-pod clause needs NO mid-resume carve-out, and entry 15 is deleted.** The staging is already correct: §7.1 closes with "The pre-attached disposition governs the pod; this reclaim governs the slot state on a pod that is released or reused rather than terminated", and SPEC-2's §7.2 step 3 states no pod outcome by design. No staged text changed.
- **§6.2's `resuming` failure-transitions subsection is self-declared authoritative, at spec/06:229, and :234 states the outcome.** ":229" declares it "the **authoritative enumeration** of every edge out of the session-model `resuming` state" and ":234" says the half-claimed replacement pod is released to the pool. An earlier design cited :230 for the first; it is :229.
- **A released half-claimed pod's residue is disposed of in BOTH pool configurations by shipped text.** Non-recycling: spec/06:80 projects a claim deleted on a `recycle.enabled: false` pod through `draining` to `terminated`. CORRECTED (`spec.8.fix-G2.1`): that spec/06:80 clause is the over-narrow one SPEC-4 now corrects, because the code retires such a pod on either recycle setting. The conclusion survives and the citation is now to the corrected clause. Recycling: spec/05:453 runs the whole-pod scrub whenever occupancy reaches zero before reuse, and `podsession/binder.go:498-501` (the `Recycle` field doc comment) records that trigger on the release path.
- **The occupancy-zero recycle boundary already waits for slot cleanup.** spec/06:175 reads "When occupancy reaches zero **and all slot cleanup has finished**, the pod leaves `claimed`", which kills the "the hold deregisters the entry, occupancy hits zero, and the whole-pod scrub races the per-slot cleanup" dress before it is built.
- **§15.4 now PUBLISHES the hold refusal's status code, and the older refutation is stale.** The staged block reads "refused with the gRPC status code `ABORTED`, which is the transient classification a caller retries on". `grep -rn ABORTED spec/ docs/ schemas/` is empty, so this is the corpus's first spec-level gRPC status on the adapter surface; the only §15.4 precedent is the `UNIMPLEMENTED` mention in the SDK-warm demotion paragraph (spec/15:1469). `codes.Aborted` is already live on the same service for the busy checkpoint op lock (checkpoint.go:115, oplock.go:37), on a disjoint RPC set, so there is no collision.
- **§7.2 orders the step-3 reclaim BEFORE step 4's `coordination_generation` bump** (spec/07:213 then :214), so the compensating `Shutdown` carries the generation the pod still holds and the bump cannot fence it.
- **A §7.4 mid-session upload refused by the hold surfaces to the client as HTTP 502 `UPSTREAM_ERROR`,** on both the `PrepareWorkspace` and the `FinalizeWorkspace` arm, so the staged "the transient classification a caller retries on" holds for that caller too. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload_to_session.go:70,:126-137.
- **`superseded` is already bound elsewhere and does not collide.** It is a `manifest_reason` enum value in the §10.1 partial-checkpoint model (spec/10:148,:157; spec/16:198; spec/29:969), a different enum on a different message.
- **"Registry entry" and "the adapter's slot registry" are already spec vocabulary** (spec/16:188, spec/28:861), so SPEC-5's heavy use of the term mints nothing undefined.
- **§28.7's wire-contract artifact register is one row per ARTIFACT under `schemas/`,** derived from the directory rather than per message or field (spec/28:1759-1765,:1781-1783), so SCHEMA-1's nine additive fields and one enum add no §28.7 row.
- **Four tier-0 proto gates were checked against SCHEMA-1 and none fires.** `claim_register_proto_agreement_test.go` inspects only rows whose `surface` contains `schemas/lenny-adapter.proto` and both staged rows name Go files; `adapter_proto_message_scope_test.go` keys on the `SessionId`/`session_id` pairing; `adapter_proto_generation_scope_test.go` counts `operationalFenceSentence` occurrences at twelve and no staged comment carries it; `adapter_session_address/session_address_wire_test.go` checks `session_id` presence and `reserved 4`. The one gate that DOES fire is the tier-3 `TestShutdownMessagePostRemovalDescriptor_spec_4_1`.
- **S10 declares `Tiers 0, 1, 3, 7a, 9`,** which is what closed the tier-3 step-ordering finding (the tier-3 suite is titled for SCHEMA-1, CODE-1 and CODE-6, and only S8 and S9 named tier 3 before). Any later edit that moves the two `Shutdown` outcome cases must move that tier 3 with them.
- **`recycle.maxSessionsPerPod` retirement is advanced by `sessionsServed`, which `RecordSessionScrub` increments,** so SPEC-3's withheld report genuinely does not advance pod retirement for a bind abandoned after `receiving_uploads`, even though that bind ran `RunSetup` and wrote a credential file. Judged "less strict rather than a control regression": the design states it and §5.2 scrub step 0's credential purge is the backstop. A reopening must argue from residue step 0 does not reach.
- **The threshold-retune scope call has a third ground, and its citations moved.** The shipped code CONFORMS to spec here (spec/06:160's rolling-window failures plus persistent leaks reaching `ceil(maxConcurrentSessions/2)` is what the clamped `(maxConcurrent+1)/2` computes), so a defects row would have no divergence to cite. Current citations: the Non-goals bullet at summary.md:315-316, the "Faster pod churn" bullet at non-spec-changes.md:1954-1963, `UnhealthyThreshold` at slothealth.go:215, `Tracker.Unhealthy` at :136, the sole production trigger at start.go:2858. The `:1764-1773`, `:214-220` and `:2857` citations in the earlier firings have drifted.

- **The `running` boundary contradiction is CLOSED, by a third route neither earlier option named.** The fix deleted the word "acknowledge(ment)" from the three START-handover sites, restated the boundary in the fence trigger's own vocabulary, and a later pass then deleted the attribution clause "which is the moment the trigger above names" as well, so SPEC-4's paragraph states the boundary on its own authority. The fence annotation at spec/06:152 and its mirror at docs/reference/state-machines.md:235 stay untouched, DOCS-1 keeps its single added row, and CODE-3's doc comment and DOCS-1's row already read arrival. The tree's boundary is `Runtime.Start` RETURNING followed by `noteRuntimeStarted`; there is no runtime-side acknowledgement of a start anywhere in `pkg/adapter` (the "runtime acknowledgement" hits in spec/04, spec/07 and spec/15 are the unrelated interrupt / CH-RUNTIMEOPS mechanism). Seven lenses across three rounds re-derived the old contradiction; do not reopen it. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/resume.go:140-144; runtimegeneration.go:36-44; server.go:348-351.
- **"Acknowledged" still carries a second, correct sense, and it must not be swept.** Every surviving use in the staging outside those three sites means "the ADAPTER did not answer the compensating `Shutdown`", which is load-bearing and untouched. A grep-and-replace over "acknowledg*" breaks the leak predicate.
- **Every gateway `PrepareWorkspace` call carries at least one upload frame, on BOTH callers.** `stageWorkspace` sends the RPC only inside `if len(uploads) > 0` (binder.go:1323-1330) and the §7.4 mid-session path is fed by `parseUploadToSession`, which 400s on `len(req.Files) == 0` (upload_to_session.go:183-186); `sendUpload` emits at least one frame per upload (adapterclient/client.go:267-286). So the upload-free stream that would report a zero epoch is unreachable from production, and the proposal's justification, which names only `stageWorkspace`, under-states a conclusion that holds on both. Eight lenses re-derived this; do not re-run it.
- **DECISION: the `ExcludePods` placement append reads `sbe.Leaked` alone; the accounting discriminator keeps `sbe.Leaked || relErr != nil`.** `relErr` is the gateway-side `ReleaseSlotReservation` failure, a Redis counter and `SandboxClaim` rollback that sends the pod no RPC and leaves no residue on it, so §5.2's placement rule, stated in pod-residue terms, does not reach it; `ClaimSlot` places only on a genuinely free slot. Both conditions still leak occupancy, so the ACCOUNTING composite is required and must not be collapsed. ALTERNATIVES rejected: widening the staged §5.2 sentence to name a failed reservation release (exports gateway bookkeeping into a reader-facing placement rule and cascades through the edge-case list), and recording the divergence as accepted (the divergence was the finding).
- **There is a SECOND tier-0 register a new test file must enter, and it has THREE derived rules, not two.** `tests/tier0_static/spec_map_slot_address_registration_test.go` holds a hand-written `slotAddressCaseFiles` inventory, and `TestTheCreditInventoryCarriesEveryAddressGuardCaseFile` fails for any `_test.go` under cmd/, migrations/, pkg/, scripts/, sdks/ or tests/ that matches rule 1, `slotSurfaceCallRE` = `slotstate\.|ClaimSlot\(|ReleaseSlot\(|ReserveSlotOnPod\(|claimAtCreate\(|BindReservedSlot\(` over the BODY; rule 2, `slotSubjectFileRE` = `(slot|one_session_only|sole_session)[^/]*_test\.go$` over the BASENAME (the `[^/]*` bars a slash, so this is a basename rule; a mid-window shard that read it as the whole repo-relative path was corrected by four later shards); or rule 3, a file under `tests/tier11_docs/` whose body mentions `TEST-GAPS.md` (:1180-1186, applied at :1217-1226). Both regexps are case-sensitive and anchored on the `(`, so the adapter's own `claimSessionSlot(`, `releaseSessionSlot(`, `reclaimSlotLocked(` and even `ReleaseSlotReservation(` do not match. An EXTRA inventory entry is never a gate failure — only omission is; `tests/tier10_conformance/recycle_scrub_conformance_test.go` sits in the inventory and matches no rule. The gate is exhaustive against the tree today, so an omission goes red on the first tier-0 run. EVIDENCE: :352, :1108-1131, :1168, :1173, :1180-1186, :1191, :1212-1230.
- **`tests/tier4_integration/concurrent_workspace_test.go` is ALREADY in `slotAddressCaseFiles`.** That is why S12 owes it whole-file spec-map credits under 7.1 and 4.7 rather than an inventory row. Do not re-file the inventory half. EVIDENCE: the tier-4 block of tests/tier0_static/spec_map_slot_address_registration_test.go.
- **Entering `slotAddressCaseFiles` is not free, and the entry must land in the SAME commit as the file.** `TestSlotAddressCasesAreCreditedToEverySectionTheyAnnotate` then demands a `tests/spec-map.json` credit under EVERY section the file's own `// spec:` annotation names, and `TestAddressCaseNamesAgreeWithTheirOwnCitations` demands the `_spec_X_Y` name suffix agree with that annotation. An inventory entry for a file that does not exist yet dies in `repoFileBytes`'s `t.Fatalf`, and an existing matching file with no entry fails the omission check, so splitting the two across commits is red in both directions. Of this proposal's new files only the two `slot*`-named ones match; `pkg/adapter/bindepoch_test.go`, `tests/tier3_contract/adapter_bind_epoch/` and `tests/tier10_conformance/bind_epoch_conformance_test.go` do not.
- **`pkg/adapter/bindepoch.go` and `pkg/adapter/bindepoch_test.go` owe no register edit.** `_test.go` files are outside the change-graph completeness domain (`changegraph.IsSourceFile` returns false), `pkg/adapter/` is already a coverage-baseline directory prefix, and `validateTestFilesMapped` walks only `componentAndAboveTierDirs()` (tier 2 and above), so a tier-1 package test under `pkg/` needs no spec-map entry. Three separate gate worries, all dead.
- **`tracing.Category` does not exist; the type is `tracing.ErrorCategory`.** The proposal said the former and it was corrected. The category is readable from an in-package tier-1 test with no new fixture: `tracing.RecordError` attaches it as the `error.category` span attribute (`AttrErrorCategory`), and `pkg/adapter/tracing_internal_test.go` already ships `installInternalSpanRecorder` and `endedSpanNamed` in package `adapter`.
- **The three `tracing.CategorizeError(..., tracing.CategoryPermanent)` stamps CODE-6 must displace are at staging.go:81, :183 and :339,** and all three handlers wire `spanErr` into a deferred `tracing.RecordError`. Asserting `slotResolveCategory` in isolation pins the helper and NOT that those three literal stamps moved, which is exactly the ship-it-broken path; the assertion belongs on the handler's recorded span. The other two resolve sites (slotsession.go:75-78, slotcreds.go:26-28) stamp no category at all, so the asymmetry is real and is named in the staging.
- **The tier-4 datastore-crossing case's trigger is an UNANSWERED compensating `Shutdown`, not one the adapter refuses.** After the amendment a refusal is `SUPERSEDED`/`ABSENT`, which CODE-4 maps to `leaked=false`, the opposite of both assertions the case exists for; the unanswered form takes the `err != nil` arm. The fixture needs no new harness: `recycleAdapterDialer` builds its client through `adapterclient.Dial`, which is variadic over `grpc.DialOption`, so a unary client interceptor keyed on the `Shutdown` RPC drops in.
- **`isTransientPodClaimError` has exactly ONE production caller, `holdOrFailOnResumeError` (start.go:3610), reached only from `handleResume`.** So CODE-5's `codes.Aborted` arm cannot widen any other classification, cannot touch the start path, and cannot be reached from `treerecovery.go`'s `resumeOnPod` caller. `status` and `codes` are already imported at start.go.
- **`status.Code` walks the wrap chain in this tree's grpc.** v1.80.0's `Code` → `Convert` → `FromError` falls back to `errors.As` for `GRPCStatus()`, verified in the module cache by four lenses, so the `codes.Aborted` arm fires through `fmt.Errorf("%w")` plus `*SlotBindError`. Do not re-derive it; do not reach for an `errors.Is` against the adapter-local sentinel, which is unexported and does not survive gRPC.
- **`recordSlotFailure` has exactly five call sites and all five are inside `materializeSlot`.** `pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/binder.go`, which holds `Binder.Resume`, has zero, so the §7.3 re-attach can move the leak gauge, trip the threshold and increment `lenny_slot_pod_replacement_total` with no `lenny_slot_failure_total` series on that pod at all. Any proposal sentence grounding a non-goal in "the counter already labels the stages at exactly these call sites" is false at the third compensation call site.
- **`resumeOnPod`'s two branches, with line numbers.** The snapshotless resume-rebuild ends at start.go:3978 and reaches `applySlotRetryPolicy` through `startOnPod` → `bindConcurrentSlot`; the checkpoint-restore branch begins at :3980, calls `s.podBinder.Resume` at :4005 and is followed immediately by `if err != nil { return "", err }` at :4041-4043 with NO retry loop. Any sentence giving the client-driven §7.3 resume a retry budget before `holdOrFailOnResumeError` is describing the other branch.
- **`releaseSessionSlot` calls NO `Runtime.Close`.** Its whole body is `deregisterSlot` → `removeSlotTree` → `cancelPodMCPIfRuntimeIdle` (slotsession.go:214-220). Only `Shutdown` (session.go:263) and the §10.1.4 `terminateHeldSession` (holdstate.go:243) close the runtime. It is also fully synchronous, which is what makes the hold it opens end before the requesting RPC answers.
- **`onHoldTimeout`'s pass 2 shares ONE 10-second context across every member, not ten seconds each,** and it loops with no early exit, so the holds pass 1 takes for all members are bounded in aggregate rather than multiplied by N. A future `continue` or `return` inside that loop would leak every skipped member's hold for the life of the pod.
- **`coordinatorHoldAllowedMethods` has FIVE entries, not three:** `CoordinatorFence`, `NegotiateVersion`, `AdapterEvents` and both `grpc.health.v1` methods (holdstate.go:52-58). `Shutdown` is genuinely refused there, so the accepted-failure-mode bullet naming "the fence, version negotiation, the event stream, and the health probes" is accurate.
- **`tests/claim-map.json` is exactly 76 rows today: 32 WIRED, 24 UNWIRED, 20 ABSENT.** Count it with python over the JSON rather than by grep. SCHEMA-1's two WIRED rows take the denominator to seventy-eight and the WIRED count to thirty-four; 0080 §1.12 and §1.18 both quote "seventy-six", and the consolidated impacts row names only §1.12's.
- **DOCS-2 verifies end to end against the tree and the gate.** Its anchors resolve (`adapter-contract.md:10` self-description, :64 `DemoteSDK` row, :75 `Shutdown` row, :84 `**Scrub responsibilities.**`), its replacement row carries all four substrings `TestAdapterContractNamesTheShutdownRPCUnderItsWireName` requires plus the two the staged tier-11 extension adds, and the added block carries the lowercase "bind epoch" and "reclaim hold" the page-level assertions need. Its `DemoteSDK` claim is true in the tree. Three other tier-11 gates read that page with `requireNoneContain` and none of their forbidden strings appears in the staged text.
- **`Binder.DialAdapter` is an injected dialer (binder.go:59) and `adapterclient.Dial` is variadic over `grpc.DialOption`,** so any tier-4 or tier-3 case that needs to fail, delay or record an adapter RPC installs an interceptor without touching production wiring.
- **The widened-function test-caller surface is exactly seven files, and one grep finds them.** `grep -rn "ensureSlotPaths\|claimSessionSlot\|resolvePrepareStagingDir\|noteRuntimeStarted\|claimPodMCPStart" --include=*_test.go .` returns `pkg/adapter/{adapterevents,export,exportpaths,one_session_only,podmcp_arming_internal,slotsession,usage}_test.go` and nothing else; all five identifiers are unexported so no caller exists outside package `adapter`. The r2 fix added the two that were missing (`exportpaths_test.go`, `one_session_only_test.go`) to the `Tests:` bullet, which closes the standing Open. Note `socketruntime_test.go` is in that bullet for a different reason, so the bullet is not the widened-caller set.
- **`one_session_only_test.go` holds SEVEN `claimSessionSlot` call sites (:91, :94, :125, :159, :194, :209, :212), not nine.** Two shards wrote nine; the staged clause deliberately carries no count.
- **`podRegistry.Put` has exactly three production callers** (start.go:2936, :4057, cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go:249), and the third is the coordinator-handoff re-adopt, which dials fresh and sends `CoordinatorFence` as its only RPC, so CODE-6's zero-latch carve-out is accurate.
- **Rule S-2's covered handler files are `session.go`, `lifecycle.go`, `checkpoint.go`, `coordination.go`, `credentials.go`, `slotcreds.go`, `attach.go`, `sdkwarm.go` plus the two renamed channel files.** This proposal opens exactly three of them, so "three of S-2's covered handler files" is right; `staging.go`, `slot.go`, `slotsession.go`, `resume.go`, `holdstate.go` and `runtimegeneration.go` are not covered. S-2's "every in-flight `pkg/adapter` handler edit must have merged first" reads inverted against S8 preceding S9-S11 and is not: putting S8 first is what guarantees none of this proposal's handler edits is in flight.
- **spec/04:1538 says `AssignCredentialsResponse` carries a `leaseToken` field, and the message is `{}`.** Already false before this proposal; SCHEMA-1 adding `bind_epoch = 1` neither creates nor worsens it. At least four lenses have met it beside the SCHEMA-1 table and had to rule it out.
- **`cleanupTimeoutSeconds` is not a CRD field anywhere in the tree,** despite §5.2 stating its floor as a "CRD validation rule" the `SandboxWarmPool` admission webhook enforces. `grep -rn cleanupTimeoutSeconds charts/ pkg/apis/` is empty. Pre-existing and unedited by SPEC-3.
- **The §5.2 reclaim hold's "or resolve" arm is VACUOUS for every RPC, and that is the fastest kill for the whole "the hold refuses X" family.** The hold begins at the critical section that DEREGISTERS the entry, so while it runs no entry exists and nothing can resolve one; the only reachable arm is creation, and the creation sites are exactly the bind sequence. It is why §15.4's "`Shutdown` is not held" is a clarification rather than an exception, and why `Attach`, `Interrupt`, `Checkpoint`, `ReportUsage` and the three credential RPCs are untouched. Refuted at least eight times.
- **§15.4's "Current protocol version: `1.0.0`" is not an obligation this proposal breaches.** Nothing in the spec requires a bump on an additive change, and `.claude/rules/code-best-practices.md` bars backward-compatibility handling outright because the platform is pre-deployment, so a deliberate semantic conformance break at an unchanged version string is not a finding. Recorded as looked-at rather than open.
- **Tier 9 and tier 10 both drive the exported `adapter.Server` directly in this repo,** with a fake runtime and fake scrub host ops rather than a runtime binary over JSONL, so CONF-1's filesystem assertions and the tier-9 credential-fence arms are observable at the tiers they are filed under, and parking inside `Runtime.Close` to assert an `ABORTED` refusal is mechanically available at tier 10.
- **There are TWO independent spec-map gates and the proposal now discharges both.** `validate-maps` (`cmd/lenny-test/cmd_validate.go:716`, `validateTestFilesMapped`) requires every `_test.go` under `componentAndAboveTierDirs()` to resolve to a spec-map entry, and a `dir/...` glob covers a whole directory. `TestSlotAddressCasesAreCreditedToEverySectionTheyAnnotate` (tests/tier0_static/spec_map_slot_address_registration_test.go:970) separately requires, for every file already in `slotAddressCaseFiles`, that `tests/spec-map.json` credit every section that file's own `// spec:` annotations name. The second gate is invisible from `validate-maps` and fires on a NEW CASE in an EXISTING file. Eight lenses across four rounds converged on this.
- **The credit gate's mode is per FILE, one row flips it, and a whole-file credit still counts in per-case mode.** `specMapCredits` (:824-858) puts a `path::TestName` row into `perCase` and every other covering entry into `wholeFile`; the gate takes the whole-file branch when `len(perCase) == 0` and the per-case branch otherwise, so a single per-case row switches every case in that file into per-case checking, forever. `creditsMissing` (:950-963) accepts `wholeFile[key] || perCase[fn][key]`, consulting `wholeFile` in BOTH branches, so the per-case branch is WEAKER than the whole-file branch and adding the first per-case row to a whole-file-credited file cannot turn tier 0 red. SETTLED (this closes the standing Open and the Trap that recorded the disagreement, re-derived by at least six shards): a whole-file credit DOES satisfy a case in a per-case-registered file, which is why `slotclaimer_test.go`'s §5.2-only tier-2 cases owe nothing even though that file carries nine per-case rows.
- **A `<dir>/...` entry counts as a whole-file credit.** `specMapEntryCoversFile` (:811-819) resolves directory globs, so a check written as `entry == path` misses them and manufactures false "this file has no credits" findings. The live globs that matter here: `pkg/adapter/...` under 4.7 only, `pkg/gateway/...` under 4.1, `pkg/gateway/sessionserver/...` under 11.4, `tests/tier4_integration/...` under 15.1.
- **The per-file credit census, with globs resolved.** `slotretry_test.go` whole {4.1, 11.4, 15.1} plus fifteen per-case rows; `slotclaimer_test.go` whole {4.1, 5.2} plus per-case; `recycle_scrub_path_test.go` whole {15.1} plus per-case; `slotbinder_test.go` whole {4.1, 4.6.3, 4.7, 5.2, 6.2, 7.1}, no per-case; `slotsession_test.go` whole {4.7 via the subtree, 4.9, 5.2, 6.1, 6.4, 15.4}, no per-case; `concurrent_workspace_test.go` whole {5.2, 6.4, 15.1, 28.5.3}, no per-case; `start_test.go` whole {4.1, 11.4} and outside the inventory. Two shards published different sets (`slotretry_test.go` as {15.1}, `slotbinder_test.go` without 4.1); that is the bare-path artifact rather than a disagreement, and the resolved sets above are the ones every later shard reproduced.
- **DECISION: the tier-4 concurrent-workspace case is `TestAbandonedBindLeavesNoSlotResidueOnTheSharedPod_spec_7_1`,** annotated `// spec: §7.1; §5.2; §4.7`, with the late-reclaim arm as a subtest of that same function and WHOLE-FILE spec-map credits for `tests/tier4_integration/concurrent_workspace_test.go` under 7.1 and 4.7 staged at S12. ALTERNATIVES rejected: per-case `path::TestName` rows (one such row flips the whole file into per-case mode and re-attributes the shipped cases and file-header annotations); folding the assertions into the shipped `TestConcurrentWorkspacePerSlotExecution_spec_5_2` (the only tier-4 case pinning §7.1's reclaim and §4.7's `SUPERSEDED` answer would then be credited to neither, and `lenny-test --spec 7.1` would not select it); copying the sibling's `§7.1; §5.2; §6.2` (this case asserts no §6.2 edge and does assert §4.7).
- **DECISION: the CODE-6 tier-1 contradiction was closed by deleting "plus cases in the two existing files below".** It contradicted the same subsection's "all of them land in the one new file" and had no resolvable referent; naming `slotsession_test.go` as a CODE-6 case home instead was rejected because that file carries no §7.1 credit.
- **DECISION: SPEC-3's leak-accounting sentence is scoped to the cleanup a reclaiming `Shutdown` performs,** with one added sentence naming the whole-pod scrub as where the handler-run cleanup's residue is accounted. The sentence was false only in naming one carrier for a cleanup that has two performers, and the second performer cannot be given a carrier without an adapter-side residue record plus a fourth reclaim outcome on the wire. ALTERNATIVES rejected: inverting CODE-4's `ABSENT` arm to leaked (`ABSENT` is the common answer on three branches and a leaked verdict drains a pod at `maxConcurrentSessions: 2`; it is also the inversion the hand amendment already undid); a fourth `SlotReclaimOutcome`; returning the error from `releaseSessionSlot` to its callers; deleting the sentence. CORRECTED in the same pass: the scrub does NOT unconditionally retire the pod on a failed verification, because `onScrubFailure` defaults to `warn`, which returns the pod to the pool with a `scrub_warning` annotation; only `fail` or reaching `maxScrubFailures` terminates it (spec/05:483,:484,:490). The staged text says "disposes of the pod under the `onScrubFailure` policy stated below".
- **The whole-pod scrub is a RECYCLING-pod mechanism,** running when occupancy reaches zero on a recycling pod before reuse (spec/05:453,:456). Any claim that the scrub bounds a residue owes the non-recycling half, where the pod retires at the same boundary. The staged edge-case bullet states both.
- **`releaseSessionSlot`'s production callers are the start-path rollbacks plus `DemoteSDK`:** session.go:133,:147,:157; resume.go:69,:73,:89,:107,:126,:134,:141; sdkwarm.go:236,:241,:251 and :298. The `sdkwarm.go:298` site is `DemoteSDK` rather than a rollback and is the odd one out. UNVERIFIED: one shard counts fourteen and another sixteen with neither correcting the other; the enumeration above is what both were reading, so a round that needs the count should recount rather than cite either.
- **DECISION: CODE-5's "Where it is read" bullet splits the two axes.** `BindSlot` stays by value, so the `ExcludePods` pointer threading touches no implementor of `slotBinder`; CODE-4's `leaked bool` widening of `ReleaseSlotReservation` DOES change the interface, both fakes take the fourth parameter at S12, and `fakeSlotBinder.released` widens from `[][2]string` to a record carrying pod, slot id and disposition. CODE-4's call-site table gained a row naming `slotretry_test.go`'s `fakeSlotBinder` and `slotretry_load_test.go`'s `concurrentSlotBinder`. This is the fix for the sentence four filings could not close; `concurrentSlotBinder` records nothing by design and needs the parameter and nothing else.
- **A live tier-11 gate reads the §4.7 `Shutdown` row and SPEC-1's replacement survives it.** `tests/tier11_docs/recycle_scrub_trigger_consistency_test.go:65,:75-100` requires that row to carry "recycle disposition", "ReportPodScrub", backticked `podId`, `cleanupCommands` and `cleanupTimeoutSeconds`, "does not block the response on the scrub", and the `05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md#52-...` link. SPEC-1 replaces only the row's opening and leaves the remainder standing, which is where all but the link live; the staged opening carries the link itself. S2's tier list already names tier 11. Nobody in the non-spec loop had recorded this gate.
- **`Binder.ClaimSlot` closes its connection after the §15.5 handshake and never reaches `materializeSlot`,** so CODE-4's call-site table is right to give its connect-stage release `false` and there is no fourth compensation site hiding there. EVIDENCE: slotbinder.go:128-134,:157-190.
- **The post-bind `registerBinding` failure is NOT a §7.1 reclaim gap.** `rollbackBinding` routes a slot-bearing result to `podBinder.ReleaseSlot`, which is the call that already sends the adapter its `Shutdown`, so the obligation's "ends when that attempt succeeds" boundary leaves no unreclaimed window. EVIDENCE: start.go:3166-3179.
- **`resumeOnPod`'s new `accountSlotFailure` caller cannot double-account.** The snapshotless resume-rebuild returns from the `startOnPod` branch (start.go:4020-4023), which already accounted through `applySlotRetryPolicy`; the new call sits on the checkpoint-restore `podBinder.Resume` branch alone, where `match` and every collaborator is in scope.
- **On a NON-recycling concurrent pool the occupancy-zero release retires the pod.** The claim DELETE plus §4.6.1's occupancy projection moves it claimed → draining, so the edge-case bullet's "a pod that does not recycle retires at that boundary and takes the residue with it" is true and the cross-tenant credential-residue finding it looks like does not exist. `Binder.ReleaseSlot`'s own doc comment ("the claim is deleted so the pod returns to the pool") is the loose wording that makes it look otherwise.
- **`AssignCredentialsResponse` is constructed inside `assignCredentialsSlot` (slotcreds.go:52),** not in the `AssignCredentials` handler (credentials.go:65), which only delegates. That is what lets the files-touched list name `slotcreds.go` for the epoch on that response, leave `credentials.go` unopened, and keep the S-2 covered-handler count at three.
- **`sectionsFromLines` reads `// spec:` annotation blocks ONLY** — never a bare `§X.Y` in prose, a `// diagnosis:` line or an assertion string (:679-693,:797-805). So DOCS-2's tier-11 extension adds no credit obligation even though it asserts §4.7.1 and §15.4 content on a file credited {4.1, 4.7, 5.2, 15.4.3, 28.5.3}. A later fix round that WIDENS that annotation turns tier 0 red.
- **`adapter_proto_generation_scope_test.go` counts EXACT occurrences of one sentence** (`operationalFenceSites = 12`) over the normalized proto text (:130-137,:186-190). A SCHEMA-1 field comment that copies a neighbouring field's generation sentence breaks the count and turns tier 0 red. The staged comments do not.
- **The golden-bytes case survives SCHEMA-1.** `TestShutdownRequestUnsetRecycleWireIdentical_spec_4_7` pins an exact byte string with everything but fields 1-3 unset, and proto3 emits nothing for a zero `int64`, so `expected_bind_epoch = 0` adds no bytes. EVIDENCE: tests/tier3_contract/gatewaycontrol_scrub/shutdown_recycle_wire_test.go:43-83.
- **§5.2's CRD webhook makes the 5s floor unreachable on an admissible concurrent pool.** It rejects any pool with `cleanupTimeoutSeconds < maxConcurrentSessions × 5`, so `slotCleanupBudget` is always `cleanupTimeoutSeconds / maxConcurrentSessions` and at least 5s, and larger on a generous pool. The proposal's repeated "an unset pool yields the 5s floor" framing understates the typical budget; at the spec's own canonical pool (`cleanupTimeoutSeconds: 60`) it is 15s at concurrency 4.
- **The per-pool claim FIFO is strictly head-of-line serialized.** Only the ticket at the head re-enters `attempt(ctx)`, and the next waiter is admitted by `yield` or `dequeue`, both of which run after `attempt` returns, so latency added inside a bind attempt is multiplied across every queued request for that pool. EVIDENCE: start.go:2606; queue.go:191,:205,:220,:240-280. This is a different mechanism from the refuted "`ExcludePods` head-of-line-blocks the pool FIFO" dress, which was about a waiter that yields to the tail.
- **`applySlotRetryPolicy`'s exhausted return is `SlotFailedError`, never an exhaustion sentinel,** so nothing downstream re-enters the queue. `runWithQueue` re-enters only on `ErrNoIdlePod`, `ErrNoConcurrentSlot` or `ErrTenantMismatch` (queue.go:103-107, gate at :143), and the refusal therefore ends the request on every pool mode including `queue`. EVIDENCE: start.go:2874-2881.
- **DECISION: the "what can meet the reclaim hold" finding was closed by prose at three sites** (the accepted-failure-mode bullet, CODE-5's "When it does not fire", summary.md's lagging-rollback residue) rather than by widening the placement predicate. ALTERNATIVES rejected: widening CODE-5's `ExcludePods` append to `ABSENT` (the common healthy answer, and CODE-4's outcome-first mapping exists to stop it reading as failure); a fourth `SlotReclaimOutcome` plus a second placement discriminator (cascades into SCHEMA-1, SPEC-5, CODE-1/4/5, tier 3 and tier 10 for a one-attempt cost); a new tier-1 case (already pinned by the conjunction of the `releaseSessionSlot`-hold case and the `Shutdown`-during-hold `ABSENT` case).
- **`reclaimSlotLocked` takes the hold only when the deregistration removed an entry,** so a compensating `Shutdown` landing BEFORE the rollback removes the entry itself, and the rollback that follows removes nothing and takes no hold. That third ordering is safe and must not be described as a hazard.
- **The real deltas of the non-spec window, recorded once.** r2 and r3 were byte-identical to r1. r3 → r4: the tier-4 case gained its name and annotation, S12's checklist line gained its spec-map sentence, the CODE-6 tier-1 preamble dropped "plus cases in the two existing files below", and the spec-map bullet gained the `concurrent_workspace_test.go` paragraph. r4 → r5: the third hold producer, six previously unnamed test functions, the tier-2 annotation narrowed from `§7.1; §5.2` to `§5.2`, and the rewritten `tests/spec-map.json` bullet. r5 → r6: the `f4` firing and the 0080 impacts row. r6 → r7: one cell of that row (the claim-register counts, 76 → 78 rows and 32 → 34 WIRED). Nothing else moved in seven rounds.
- **The §4.7 RPC tables live INSIDE §4.7.1.** The heading is spec/04:659, the Gateway→Adapter table runs :665-686, the Adapter→Gateway table opens :688 and closes before `#### 4.7.2` at :695. SPEC-1 (the `Shutdown` row opening) and SPEC-5 (the insert after the second table) therefore edit two anchors in one subsection without colliding. A reviewer who reads "§4.7" and "§4.7.1" as different sections files a false collision.

The choice of `ABORTED` is a restatement rather than a mint on the gateway side.
- **The §10.1 coordinator hold publishes `UNAVAILABLE` with a `coordinator_hold` detail** at four §28 sites (spec/28:251,:304,:377,:1813), while §15.4's reclaim hold publishes `ABORTED`. Different holds, and nothing in `spec/` maps gRPC codes to categories, so the asymmetry is not a contradiction. A future reviewer will notice it; do not file it on that basis alone.
- **§5.2's whole-pod scrub, clause by clause, so SPEC-3's backstop sentence need not be re-derived.** Step 0 removes every `/run/lenny/slots/{sessionId}/credentials.json` (spec/05:461); step 2 is `rm -rf /workspace/slots/*` (:468); step 6 stat-verifies `/workspace/slots/`, `/tmp`, `/dev/shm` and `/run/lenny/slots/` and marks the scrub failed on residue (:471); `onScrubFailure` governs the failure (:481-483). All four sit BELOW the `**Scrub model.**` paragraph at :453, so SPEC-3's "stated below" pointers are literally true. More than a dozen shards verified this; do not re-run it.
- **The whole-pod scrub enumerates `/run/lenny/slots` children ON DISK rather than from the registry** (pkg/adapter/podscrub.go:132-137, whose own comment contemplates "a leaked slot whose registry entry is already gone"), so it reaches a credential residue whose entry the adapter has already deregistered. That is what makes SPEC-3's whole-pod-boundary accounting reachable rather than notional.
- **A pod that does not recycle retires at the occupancy-zero boundary in BOTH non-recycling modes.** The §5.2 configuration table gives `maxConcurrentSessions: 1, recycle.enabled: false` as "terminates when the session ends" (spec/05:424) and the bounded cohort as "terminates after the cohort drains" (:427); spec/06:80 projects a claim deleted on a non-recycling pod through `draining` to `terminated`, and spec/06:175 makes occupancy-zero wait for slot cleanup.
- **`buf`'s lint config is inline in the ROOT `buf.yaml`** (STANDARD plus `enum_zero_value_suffix: _UNSPECIFIED`), despite that file's own comment claiming the module config lives in `schemas/buf.yaml`, which does not exist. `SLOT_RECLAIM_OUTCOME_*` satisfies ENUM_VALUE_PREFIX and the zero-value suffix rule. It cannot here: spec/04, 05, 06, 07, 15 and 29 carry no retired spelling at all; the only live ones are the naming-table rows in spec/28:149-159 and spec/17:1530.

An agent expecting a flat file there will think the entry is malformed.
- **The three deregister-then-destroy sites are exactly three, and `ShutdownDemoteSDK` is not a fourth.** `Shutdown` (session.go:238), `releaseSessionSlot` (slotsession.go:215 via `deregisterSlot` at :192) and `deregisterStartedSessions` (slotsession.go:389, reached from holdstate.go:190, destroying at :254). `ShutdownDemoteSDK` (sdkwarm.go:69) delegates to `DemoteSDK`, whose release is `releaseSessionSlot` at sdkwarm.go:298.

CODE-6's hold refusal at the top of that function therefore has no bypass.

So CODE-6's idempotent-repeat clause covers the whole early-return-before-epoch-capture hazard and no second handler needs it.

A future change that moves a revoke or rotate site onto `ensureSlotStateLocked` turns this into a fail-open; that is the thing to re-check.
- **There is NO per-session missing-report timeout.** Every missing-report timer in the tree is the §5.2 whole-pod recycle-boundary timer armed at the `bound → recycling` patch (pkg/gateway/session/recycle/recycleboundary.go:24-74), so withholding `ReportSessionScrub` on the pre-`running` path leaves no timer to fire.

- **The three spec-lane sites carrying the refuted two-ground argument were FIXED.** `[non-spec.2.fix-reclaim-hold-callers]` edited `spec-changes.md` at all three: SPEC-1's staged §7.1 replacement block, the Design section's parallel sentence, and the accepted-failure-mode bullet. The staged §7.1 paragraph now says §5.2 keeps its retries off a pod whose reclaim did not complete and that a hold no reclaim opened is outside that constraint; the bullet names the three callers that reach the hold and prices the cost at one attempt out of a budget whose default is one retry. - **Four consecutive summary-cleanup firings re-verified the same structure and moved nothing.** `f1`, `f2`, `f6` and `f7` each re-read the eight required sections in order with `## Deliverable index` last, confirmed `**Fixed decisions.**` and `**What is fixed.**` are gone, confirmed no `### Retired` block exists inside `## Open decisions for human to make`, and confirmed the section carries 11, 16, 17 and 18 with identifiers verbatim. Entry 17 is the only one carrying a recommendation; 16 and 18 record explicitly that the loop derived none; 11 states none. A fifth structural firing over unmoved text is waste.

- **DESIGN GENERATION: the bind epoch is withdrawn and the mechanism is a gateway-minted per-attempt BIND ATTEMPT TOKEN.** The 2026-09-16 hand revision (commit e53e4800a) replaced it wholesale. The gateway mints one token per bind attempt from a cryptographically secure source (16 bytes hex in the code lane), the adapter stamps it on the entry it creates, compares it for equality and never mints one of its own, and no response reports a token. Every entry below that argues in epoch vocabulary is a record of the withdrawn design.
- **The token-carrying request set is five, and it is closed.** `PrepareWorkspace`, `FinalizeWorkspace`, `RunSetup`, `AssignCredentials` and `Resume` carry `bind_attempt`; `PrepareWorkspace` and `FinalizeWorkspace` are the two that also carry `mid_session`. `StartSession` and `ConfigureWorkspace` carry NEITHER field and are governed by the started-session rule alone. Every other RPC resolves an entry the session already holds, creates none, and is outside the admission rules.
- **`mid_session` already ships on `FinalizeWorkspaceRequest` (field 4) and does NOT exist on `PrepareWorkspaceRequest`,** whose field 4 is `reserved "slot_id"`. SCHEMA-1 adds the marker to `PrepareWorkspace` only, which is why §15.4's wire sentence names one message while §4.7.1 names two. More than a dozen shards have read this backwards off a single grep hit. EVIDENCE: schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:713-724, :682-696.
- **The SCHEMA-1 field numbers under the token design, verified free by many independent lenses.** `PrepareWorkspaceRequest` `bind_attempt`=5, `mid_session`=6 (1-3 used, 4 reserved); `FinalizeWorkspaceRequest` `bind_attempt`=6 (1-4 used, 5 reserved); `RunSetupRequest` 5 (4 reserved); `AssignCredentialsRequest` 4 (3 reserved); `ResumeRequest` 16 (6 and 15 reserved); `ShutdownRequest` `bind_attempt`=7 and `unconditional_teardown`=8 (1,2,3,5,6 used, 4 reserved); `ShutdownResponse` `slot_reclaim`=3. Do not re-derive these, and do not carry the retired nine-number epoch table forward.
- **`ShutdownResponse.slot_was_started` was minted and then DELETED from the whole staging,** with the `lenny_slot_compensation_removed_started_total` row that was its only consumer. `ShutdownResponse` gains `slot_reclaim` at field 3 and nothing else.
- **Two adapter `ErrorCode` values are minted, 28 and 29, and they take NO §15.1 row.** `SLOT_BIND_ATTEMPT_SUPERSEDED` is answered on `ABORTED` with `CATEGORY_TRANSIENT`; `SLOT_BIND_ALREADY_STARTED` on `FAILED_PRECONDITION` with `CATEGORY_PERMANENT`. The precedent is `ERROR_CODE_PROTOCOL_VERSION_INCOMPATIBLE = 27` (schemas/lenny-adapter.proto:585), which is published through §15.4.2 alone (spec/15:1699) and has no §15.1 catalog row. `spec/` carries no adapter-ErrorCode catalog anywhere.
- **§15.1's catalog is the CLIENT-facing `code` table with an HTTP Status column** (spec/15:1002,:1006). A row there asserts the code reaches a client. The gateway renders no adapter `ErrorCode` into a REST envelope on any staged path, so a row for either new code would be a category error. That is the ground on which the staged §15.1 sub-block and the first DOCS-3 were deleted.
- **The `Shutdown` selector is two fields and the request must ask for exactly one.** A request carrying a `bind_attempt`, and a request carrying `unconditional_teardown`, are the two legal forms; a request carrying neither, and a request carrying both, is answered `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. Every non-bind caller (the §11.4 revoke fan-out, the occupancy-zero recycle edge, `Binder.ReleaseSlot`, `Binder.shutdownAdapter`) must set the flag, which is a code-lane obligation worth a test.
- **The admission cascade has a published ORDER, and it is: the §5.2 reclaim hold, then field well-formedness, then the rules for a request that resolves no entry, then the attempt-identity rule, then the started-session rule.** The hold and the well-formedness check both PRECEDE the cascade rather than sit inside it. The staged code evaluates in exactly that order, and §15.4 says explicitly that its clause set is not an evaluation order and points at §4.7.1 for the one that is.
- **A request meeting both the differing-token condition and the started-entry condition is refused `SLOT_BIND_ATTEMPT_SUPERSEDED`,** so a stale bind identity stays transient rather than becoming the permanent `SLOT_BIND_ALREADY_STARTED`. The overlap is reachable and the two codes carry opposite client retryability, so the tie-break is load-bearing.
- **Field well-formedness is `validateBindFields(bindAttempt string, midSession bool) error` in `pkg/adapter/slot.go`,** returning `codes.InvalidArgument` when `(bindAttempt == "") != midSession`, unwrapped, called at the entry of the five handlers whose messages carry the fields. It does NOT route through `slotResolveError`, which would re-present it as a slot-resolve failure and hand `SlotBindError.Reason()` a workspace-validation reason.
- **`StartSession` and `ConfigureWorkspace` both CREATE the entry when none exists,** through `claimSessionSlot` → `claimSessionSlotUnderLock` → `ensureSlotStateLocked`, so either can mint an UNTOKENED entry. That entry is answered `superseded` by a named reclaim (fail-closed, because it may be a live successor's), is released only by the unconditional teardown or by the pod's retirement, and refuses every later bind attempt at that session on that pod. It is a recorded accepted residue with recovery routed to position 2 of the remediation plan. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:111; sdkwarm.go:217; slotsession.go:75.
- **`FinalizeWorkspace` is UNCONDITIONAL on both bind paths and precedes the start,** so on any correct ordering the entry is always created by a token-carrying RPC and the untokened entry needs an abandoned or late start to occur. `PrepareWorkspace` is the conditional one (uploads only). EVIDENCE: podsession/binder.go:921; slotbinder.go:291.
- **`SLOT_BIND_ALREADY_STARTED` on `RunSetup` is reachable, by the interleaving §4.7.1 itself describes.** Attempt B's `PrepareWorkspace` creates the entry under B's token; abandoned attempt A's late `StartSession` carries no token, is admitted because the entry has not started, and starts the session on B's entry; B's `RunSetup` then matches on token and falls to the phase gate. Do not dismiss the setup-window envelope mapping as unreachable.
- **`writeSetupCommandError` branches on the gRPC code ALONE.** `status.Code(cause) == codes.FailedPrecondition` gives 422 `SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED` with `details.reason: "setup_command_failed"` and no `Retry-After`; every other code, `Aborted` included, gives the retryable 503 fallback with `Retry-After`. Every `RunSetup` error is wrapped in `*SetupCommandFailure` on both bind paths, and `writePodClaimError` checks the typed setup case first. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/start.go:186-194,:217-249; podsession/binder.go:933-943; slotbinder.go:299-305.
- **DECISION: §15.1's `SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED` row is WIDENED rather than the gateway's envelope selection narrowed,** because narrowing the code is outside this proposal. §15.1's exclusion sentence ("every gRPC code other than `FailedPrecondition` ... is not this code") is keyed on the CODE and survives untouched. spec/06:290's `**Client visibility:**` clause is quantified over "any other setup-window failure" and IS falsified, so it is re-keyed on the gRPC code under a new SPEC-5 anchor. spec/07:208 is a one-way cause-to-code enumeration inside a parenthetical and stays true, merely incomplete.
- **DECISION: DOCS-3 is redefined.** It was deleted once (as the two published error-catalog rows) and re-minted by `spec.6.fix-G2` as the `docs/reference/error-catalog.md:129` `SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED` row edit, stating both `FAILED_PRECONDITION` causes and qualifying the setup-output remedy, in that page's own voice with no spec section numbers. It is named in checklist S7 and is defined NOWHERE in `non-spec-changes.md`; the docs loop owes the section.
- **DECISION: the post-start entry confirmation is a PUBLISHED obligation.** It is stated once in the SPEC-5 §4.7.1 block ("**A start confirms the entry is still its own.**") and mirrored as one §15.4 non-conformance clause, (j), enforced at tier 3 and tier 10. It is stated UNIVERSALLY for every request that starts a session, so CODE-2's SDK-warm site (sdkwarm.go:261) is cascaded to refuse through its own `releaseSessionSlot` plus the §6.1 `DemoteSDK` idiom rather than discarding the bool. This CLOSES the standing Open on whether a third-party adapter omitting the confirmation conforms: it does not.
- **DECISION: the `ExcludePod(s)` placement mechanism is WITHDRAWN WHOLE, and no placement rule survives anywhere in the staging.** `grep ExcludePod` over the staging returns nothing outside the review logs. What governs a retry meeting the reclaiming pod is the per-attempt token compare (a surviving entry) or the reclaim hold (a running cleanup), and both refusals are transient. §5.2's `**Max retries:**` bullet is left exactly as it stands.
- **§5.2's retry placement prefers the SAME pod.** "The retry is always assigned to a **new slot** on the same pod (if a slot is available) or on a different pod from the pool (if the original pod is fully saturated or unhealthy)" (spec/05:555). `applySlotRetryPolicy` re-submits the identical `req` with no exclusion and `maxSlotRetries == 1`, so a same-pod refusal is the request's last attempt.
- **`WARM_POOL_EXHAUSTED` is the claim-exhaustion code and is NOT §5.2's retry-budget exhaustion.** It is reached only through the `ErrNoConcurrentSlot`/`ErrNoIdlePod`/`ErrTenantMismatch` sentinels the retry loop returns unchanged; the budget exhaustion returns the structured `error.category` error of the `**Client error on exhaustion:**` bullet. Do not write `WARM_POOL_EXHAUSTED` into any retry-budget narrative.
- **DECISION: §7.1's reclaim obligation is scoped by whether the pod survives the failure, rather than by a list of call sites.** It binds the §15.1 start transition onto a slot on a pod serving concurrent sessions (reserved at creation or placed by §5.2's retry policy) and the §7.3 re-attach onto a replacement pod. `Binder.Prepare` and `Binder.Launch` drain the pod through `failPhase` and send no compensation at all, so the exclusive start discharges the obligation by retirement.
- **DECISION: SPEC-2's §7.1 atomicity-parenthetical edit is DELETED and the shipped parenthetical stands.** Session creation leaves no pod-side state to reclaim on either pool class: on an exclusive pool `Binder.Prepare`'s failure closure drains the pod, and on a concurrent pool `prepareAtFinalize` returns before any adapter RPC. SPEC-2's §7.1 block is now one inserted paragraph and no in-place edit.
- **The finalize gate is one line and it is the warrant for "no adapter RPC runs at finalize on a concurrent pool".** `finalize.go` returns `(nil, nil)` before `s.podBinder.Prepare` when `match.ExecutionMode == service || match.MaxConcurrentSessions > 1`. §7.1's own numbered flow reads as if steps 11-13 do pod I/O at finalize, and §15.1:646-647 carries the carve-out ("On a concurrent-workspace pool whose reserved slot materializes and runs setup at start"). Several lenses have lost a pass trying to falsify this.
- **`Binder.Prepare` runs at `/finalize` and `Binder.Launch` at `/start`, two independent HTTP requests, with nothing carrying in-process state between them.** `Launch` re-dials and issues only `ConfigureWorkspace` or `StartSession`. That is why neither can hold `Prepare`'s token, and it is the load-bearing reason the two starts carry none.
- **DECISION: the reclaim hold's bound is stated by chaining through §5.2's own `**Slot cleanup:**` bullet.** The two sentences arguing that the bullet's timeout is "not a second bound" and that the directory removal runs "under no deadline" are deleted. The hold lasts as long as the cleanup; on a pod serving concurrent sessions a cleanup that overruns the bullet's timeout is a failed cleanup, so the slot is `leaked` and its identifier stays held until the pod terminates. Ending the hold at the timeout was rejected as actively unsafe: releasing the identifier while `os.RemoveAll` runs readmits a successor onto a tree being deleted.
- **DECISION: §15.4's unconditional-teardown non-conformance clause is scoped to an adapter that holds an entry for the named session,** because the unscoped form declared the shipped occupancy-zero recycle `Shutdown` and the §11.4 revoke fan-out non-conforming. The no-entry case is answered `absent` by §4.7.1 and by the §4.7 row.
- **`superseded` covers TWO adapter states, not one:** an entry a different bind attempt created, and an entry carrying no token, the latter so the rule fails closed. Any definition of `superseded` as "a different bind attempt owns the identifier" is too narrow, which is why the §16.1 row now states the outcome by pointer to §4.7.1.
- **SPEC-6 stages three §16.1 rows and their content is settled.** `lenny_slot_compensation_superseded_total` (gateway, `pool` and `k8s_pod_name`, stating no expected value and no expected rate); `lenny_slot_compensation_outcome_unrecognized_total` (gateway, UNLABELLED, because its emitter is the free function `slotReclaimLeaked`, which holds neither the pool nor the pod name); `lenny_slot_shutdown_untokened_entry_total` (adapter-side, `k8s_pod_name`, with the not-scraped deferral).
- **Every metric row's expected-VALUE clause on this proposal has been wrong three times running.** "Zero on a conforming deployment", then "non-zero on a system that retries and is the count of retries the fence protected", then the same claim one step smaller. The convention §16.1 supports is to state what a series counts and stop; `lenny_slot_failure_total` and `lenny_warmpool_claims_total` carry no expectation at all.
- **The adapter DOES hold its own pod identity,** `os.Getenv("POD_NAME")` from the Downward API, cached as `s.podID` at construction, with a fail-closed withhold when it is unset. So an adapter-emitted series labelled `k8s_pod_name` is emittable. The `RecycleScrub` proto comment "The adapter, which holds no pod identity of its own" is about the claim-row key on the wire. At least eight lenses have had to settle this. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/server.go:36,:372-380; sessionscrubreporter.go:56-74.
- **The metric-catalog gates are one-directional and none is tripped by adding §16.1 rows alone.** `TestMetricCatalogIsCompleteAgainstSpec161` and `TestMetricCatalogHasNoUnspecifiedMetrics` compare `MetricCatalog()` against the HAND-MAINTAINED `spec161Metrics` slice in `catalog_test.go` rather than against spec/16; the alert cross-check runs alert-to-catalog; tier-11's `adapter_metric_catalog_test.go` runs registered-adapter-metric to both catalogs with an empty `specCatalogPending`. A §16.1 row with no registered metric fails nothing.
- **The gateway metric catalog is `pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go` plus its `spec161Metrics` list, and an ADAPTER counter must be registered in `pkg/adapter/metrics.go` instead.** `pkg/gateway/observability/catalog.go`, which CODE-9 names twice, does not exist. An adapter counter registered gateway-side silently escapes the tier-11 adapter sweep.
- **§16.1 already carries adapter-process rows with a fixed house phrasing,** "emitted by the adapter process inside the agent pod and therefore outside the [§16.9](#169-prometheus-scrape-targets-and-crds) default scrape target set until a deployer wires an adapter scrape target" (spec/16:186-189). SPEC-6's third row paraphrases it without the §16.9 link. That is wording; at least six lenses have checked it and none filed it.
- **The adapter genuinely exposes no scrape endpoint.** `pkg/adapter/metrics.go` registers into `prometheus.DefaultRegisterer` and no `promhttp` handler exists under `pkg/adapter/` or `cmd/lenny-adapter/`; §16.9's canonical target set is gateway, controller, token service and `lenny-ops`. So SPEC-6's deferral claim is true, and agent-pod ingress is `allow-gateway-ingress` only, so nothing in §13.2 contradicts it.
- **`k8s_pod_name` is sanctioned and raises no cardinality question.** §16.1.1 is the single-source-of-truth table for attribute NAMES and carries it; `lenny_slot_failure_total` and `lenny_slot_pod_replacement_total` already pair it with `pool`; the forbidden list is `session_id`, `operation_id`, `root_session_id`, `credential_id`, `user_id`, `lease_id`, `request_id`. Its "Used on" column is descriptive and no gate parses it.
- **Both gateway leak ledgers share ONE `slothealth.Tracker` instance.** `cmd/lenny-gateway/sessiondeps.go:261` mints it and the same pointer reaches both `sessionsrv.go`'s `SlotHealth` (the `applySlotRetryPolicy`/`accountSlotFailure` path) and `newScrubReportService(..., Tracker: ...)` (the `drainLedger.RecordLeak` path). A finding shaped "the withheld report lands in a different ledger than §6.2's trigger evaluates" dies here. What genuinely differs is the GAUGE, which only the bind-failure path publishes.
- **The adapter today refuses a double start with `codes.Unavailable`, not `FailedPrecondition`,** so the staged `SLOT_BIND_ALREADY_STARTED` / `FAILED_PRECONDITION` / `CATEGORY_PERMANENT` answer is a deliberate retryability CHANGE on that path rather than a restatement of shipped behaviour. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:78-85.
- **`ConfigureWorkspace` hard-codes `idempotentRepeat = true`,** so `allowStarted` is always true there, and §4.7.1's "unless it is a repeat `ConfigureWorkspace` for the session that started on that pod" is equivalent, because the entry is keyed by session identifier. spec/04:673 publishes the idempotency and is untouched. Do not file this as predicate drift.
- **The mid-session credential refresh path is `RotateCredentials`, not `AssignCredentials`,** so the started-session rule cannot reach proactive lease renewal (spec/04:1456 step 3). The checkpoint-restart rotation path (`Checkpoint` → replacement pod → `AssignCredentials` → `Resume`) is a fresh bind attempt on a new pod and is inside the token contract rather than against it. This was the strongest unstaged-site candidate and it died here.
- **`DemoteSDK` is called at exactly one site and it runs BEFORE any entry-creating RPC of the attempt,** at the top of `Binder.Prepare` (binder.go:882), ahead of `stageWorkspace` and `FinalizeWorkspace`, and it releases the registry entry synchronously inside its own RPC. So there is no post-demotion untokened-entry route and no post-demotion hold. A `ConfigureWorkspace` failure reclaims and returns; it does not demote and retry.
- **The §4.7 RPC tables physically sit INSIDE §4.7.1** (heading spec/04:659, `Shutdown` row :686, Adapter→Gateway table opening :688, `#### 4.7.2` at :695), and citing that row as "§4.7" is the shipped repo convention. A reviewer who reads "§4.7" and "§4.7.1" as different sections files a false collision.
- **The adapter `Shutdown` caller set in the tree is FOUR functions across five sites,** and the §4.7 commentary names three. `Binder.ReleaseSlot` (slotbinder.go:542) and its recycle arm (:574), `Binder.shutdownAdapter`'s retire arm (binder.go:2043) and its recycle arm (:2037), and `cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:129`. The non-spec lane carries the full table, so nothing is orphaned; a fixer editing that commentary should add `Binder.shutdownAdapter`.
- **§28.4's claim register is the FILE `tests/claim-map.json`, seeded from `scripts/seed-claim-register.py`, rather than a table inside spec/28.** So the §15.4 commentary's promised `ABSENT` row obliges no spec/28 edit and does not contradict the "§28's registers deliberately untouched" bullet. At least six lenses have nearly filed the pair.
- **The anchor sweep under the token design is done and clean.** Every "text to replace" fenced block in `spec-changes.md` resolves exactly once across `spec/` and every "replace it with" block zero times, re-derived mechanically over all 28 fenced blocks by more than ten independent lenses. The §4.7.1 insertion window (spec/04:688-695), the §15.4 window (spec/15:1469-1471) and the §6.2 fence window (spec/06:150-156) are each unique.
- **The checklist is now TWENTY-TWO steps under the token design,** and every entry below that names S1-S14 with epoch-era contents is reading the superseded sequence. Known live step identities from this window: S1 SPEC-5, S3 SPEC-2's §7.1 paragraph, S6 SPEC-6, S7 DOCS-1/DOCS-2/DOCS-3, S9 SCHEMA-1, S13 CODE-6, S15 CODE-1, S17 CODE-2, S20 CODE-9, S22 CONF-1 plus the claim-register row.

- **DECISION: the pre-`running` cleanup trigger names its ACTORS rather than quantifying over bind
failures.** The §5.2 append, the §6.2 prose and the fence annotation now name the §7.1 pod-side
reclaim where its obligation reaches the attempt, and the adapter's own handler for a start that
fails, and state the disposition of the case neither reaches (a pre-attached bind on a pod serving
one session, whose registry entry and per-slot tree are reclaimed with the pod and which reports
nothing). ALTERNATIVES rejected: widening §7.1 to cover the pre-attached one-session bind (obliges a
`Shutdown` to a pod the pre-attached disposition already retires, and puts an adapter RPC on
`failPhase`, which issues none); giving the adapter a cleanup handler on the four
workspace/setup/credential RPCs (a new mechanism, and it collides with first-writer-wins entry
ownership); deleting the trigger sentence (leaves the report-withholding and one-report rules with
no named window).

(podsession/binder.go:1072-1082), so a pre-attached bind failing at `PrepareWorkspace`,

the `Shutdown` handler are start paths: session.go:133,:147,:157; resume.go:69-141;
sdkwarm.go:236-298; holdstate.go:254. No handler for the four workspace/setup/credential RPCs
releases the slot.
