- **MISTAKE, the same sentence failing in BOTH directions: §4.7.1's "Every other RPC on this contract neither carries nor reports ..." closure.** Written outside the `Shutdown` block it excluded `Shutdown`, and five lenses filed it; moved INTO the `Shutdown` block its "other" resolved against `Shutdown` and excluded the five bind carriers. It is now deleted and the carriage rule lives in exactly one sentence at the enumeration site ("`StartSession` and `ConfigureWorkspace` carry none, and no other request on this contract carries one"). Do not reintroduce a free-standing "every other RPC" carriage sentence in any position.
- **Do NOT delete or "harmonize" the OTHER "Every other RPC" sentence.** §4.7.1's "Every other RPC on this contract resolves an entry the session already holds, creates none, and is outside these rules" is scoped to the ADMISSION rules, sits before `Shutdown` is introduced, and is correct for the pod-scoped RPCs. The DOCS-2 paragraph uses the same admission-scoped phrasing and is likewise correct.
- **MISTAKE, two rounds on one sentence: per-RPC reachability narration inside §4.7.1's Admission paragraph.** Round 1 wrote it as a universal (which refused every `StartSession`), round 3 narrowed it to a closed enumeration, and round 3's lens filed the enumeration. It is now DELETED rather than narrowed a third time, because every rule in the cascade is self-selecting by its own condition and a per-RPC reachability claim beside them has no job. A third enumeration, however qualified, is the same answer one step smaller.
- **MISTAKE: `ShutdownResponse.slot_was_started` was the code lane's intruder into a spec lane that never admitted it.** Its only consumer anywhere was one §16.1 counter whose stated purpose was false on both halves: §7.1 obliges the compensation exactly when the adapter may have admitted the start, so `reclaimed` over a started entry is the DESIGNED outcome, and a `reclaimed` answer already means the tokens compared equal, so the counter could not observe an identity-gate failure at all. Field and row both deleted. The operational question is already carried by `lenny_slot_failure_total{error_type=...}`.
- **Do NOT flip the `Shutdown` four-way compare so a named reclaim removes an untokened entry.** "Token versus empty = reclaimed" looks like the fix for the untokened residue. An untokened entry can be the one a SUCCESSOR's own `StartSession` created for a session that is now running, so removing it tears down a live session. `superseded` there is the fail-closed answer; only its old justification ("no compensated path produces an entry carrying no token") was false.
- **Do NOT reclassify the untokened-entry `superseded` as a reclaim that "did not complete"** to get the §5.2 whole-pod replacement trigger to collect it. The same untokened entry may belong to a session the pod is legitimately serving and the gateway cannot tell; replacing that pod destroys a live session.
- **`superseded` for a tokenless entry is CORRECT on the `Shutdown` path and WRONG on the bind path.** The bind-path identity refusal fires only when the request's non-empty token differs from the entry's non-empty token. Do not sweep the phrase "carries no token" out of the `Shutdown` sites, and do not import it into the bind-path gate.
- **Do NOT put the reclaim hold back inside the admission cascade as "rule 1".** §5.2's hold reaches "any request that would create or resolve a registry entry", which is a WIDER set than the requests §4.7.1's cascade governs; a `Checkpoint` resolves an entry and is refused by the hold while §4.7.1 says it is outside the rules entirely. State the hold by reference to §5.2 as a check that precedes the cascade and carries its own scope.
- **The hold's precedence over the no-entry rule is load-bearing, not decoration.** During a hold the entry is already deregistered, so a `mid_session` `PrepareWorkspace` resolves no entry and the no-entry rule would answer the PERMANENT `FAILED_PRECONDITION` where §5.2 promises a transient refusal the caller retries.
- **"Carries `bind_attempt`" means the message TYPE declares the field, never that the value is non-empty.** The fields are bare proto3 scalars with no presence. That reading is what lets the well-formedness condition exclude `StartSession`/`ConfigureWorkspace` with no carve-out clause. Any rewrite that loses it needs the carve-out back.
- **`ensureSlotStateLocked`'s rule switch has NO field-validity case and must not grow one.** The well-formedness gate sits above it, at each handler's entry. Anybody reading the switch alone concludes the create branch fires unconditionally on an absent entry, which is correct only because a malformed request never reaches the function.
- **WATCHOUT: `mid_session` is a bare proto3 bool on a CLIENT-STREAMING message, so "omitted" and "false" are the same bytes.** A later `PrepareWorkspace` frame carrying an empty `bind_attempt` where the first carried one is explicitly not a disagreement, but a later frame whose `mid_session` is false where the first was true IS a refusal. No spec or staged-code text states the sender's obligation to repeat `mid_session` on every frame; `sendUpload` builds every frame from scratch (adapterclient/client.go:269-284), and CODE-8 is the sender.
- **MISTAKE that cost most of a pass: deriving that `superseded` is unreachable on the retry path.** The chain (a retry meeting a surviving entry is refused and creates nothing, so the lagging reclaim always compares equal) assumes the abandoned attempt created an entry. The reachable path is the attempt whose first entry-creating RPC never reached the adapter: §7.1 still owes a reclaim, the successor creates a fresh entry under its own token, and the lagging reclaim meets it and is answered `superseded`. The proposal's own bullet already contemplates it.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed twice: "§4.1's retained second sentence becomes a false field inventory once the two new `ShutdownRequest` fields land".** It dies on the tree: `ShutdownRequest` ALREADY carries `reason` (2), `deadline_ms` (3) and `coordination_generation` (6) beside the address, so the clause was never an exhaustive field list. One lens lost a full verification round to it.
- **Do NOT file §5.2:459's recycle-`Shutdown` payload list as missing `unconditional_teardown`.** It enumerates the scrub payload (recycle disposition, `podId`, `cleanupCommands`, `cleanupTimeoutSeconds`) and already omits `session_id`, `reason`, `deadline_ms` and `coordination_generation`, all shipped. §4.7's row states the caller rule generically. A filer needs an argument that the sentence is read as the complete field set.
- **Do NOT file §11.4's revoke fan-out as a missing edit site.** §11.4 enumerates no `ShutdownRequest` fields, so no §11 sentence becomes false; the obligation on that caller to set `unconditional_teardown` is carried by the §4.7 row and by the code lane. The same reasoning covers §5.2's recycle boundary sentence.
- **Do NOT file the §15.1 `SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED` cause drift a second time.** It is now staged as a widening under SPEC-5, its exclusion sentence is untouched, spec/06:290 is re-keyed on the gRPC code, spec/07:208 is a one-way enumeration that stays true, and `docs/operator-guide/troubleshooting.md:41` was adjudicated not-a-site because the remedy stays right for the setup-command cause. The row ALSO over-claims before this proposal applies, because shipped `RunSetup` answers `FailedPrecondition` for "adapter is not configured with a workspace root".
- **Do NOT file the §16.1.1 "Used on" column as going stale.** §16.1.1 is the single source of truth for attribute NAMES, both labels are already in it, the column is descriptive, and nothing mechanical parses it. Three lenses have declined it.
- **Do NOT file the `k8s_pod_name` label on SPEC-6's adapter row as unemittable.** The adapter caches the Downward API `POD_NAME`. The earlier "the site cannot supply that label" fix was about the GATEWAY free function `slotReclaimLeaked`, which holds neither the pool nor the pod name, and it must not be generalised to the adapter. At least eight lenses have rebuilt this.
- **WATCHOUT: DOCS-3 is named in the checklist and defined nowhere in `non-spec-changes.md`.** One fix group deleted it and a later one re-minted it with a different subject. If two groups are told to define DOCS-3 in one round, the definitions collide; check what the checklist's S7 line currently means before writing.
- **Do NOT file a §15.4 completeness finding, now for the fifth time.** The refuted set is the missing wire spelling of the unconditional form, the missing status code for the hold refusal, the missing per-outcome response rule, the zero-frame `PrepareWorkspace` response, and the unnamed `ErrorCode` for the hold refusal. Each dies on the same ground: a semantically-stated requirement plus a correct first-party implementation is not a correctness defect.
- **The snapshot trap fired on EVERY round of this window, in both lanes.** `spec-r2` through `spec-r6` and `non-spec-recheck-3-r4`/`-r5` were each byte-identical to the working tree, so `diff -rq` returned nothing and "read the changed sections first" had no target. More than twenty shards opened by discovering it. Run `diff -rq` across the whole snapshot chain first, take the newest snapshot that actually differs, and when every snapshot is identical use `git diff HEAD -- proposals/0081_*/`.
- **MISTAKE that burned six fix groups in one round: findings written against the pre-revision text.** The hand revision moved roughly eighty lines and rewrote whole blocks, and three groups resolved to NO EDIT because the quoted string no longer existed anywhere. Verify a finding's quoted STRING against the current file before designing a fix, and never trust its line number.
- **MISTAKE, the parallel-rewrite seam: the hand revision edited three files at once and left the defects BETWEEN them.** SPEC-6 staged a metric depending on a `ShutdownResponse` field the spec staging never published; SPEC-2 kept a §5.2 placement edit whose mechanism the code lane had withdrawn; CODE-6's call-site table read a `req.GetBindAttempt()` that SCHEMA-1 does not add to `StartSessionRequest`. When reviewing after a multi-file revision, read ACROSS the spec and non-spec files rather than down either one.
- **MISTAKE: a scope call resting on the shipped tree when a staged deliverable moves it.** Recorded again this window in the §5.2 `**Max retries:**` residue and in the "only production call site is `applySlotRetryPolicy`" attribution. Check the ground against the STAGED tree.

- **Do NOT split the pre-`running` cleanup trigger by POD CLASS.** "On a pod serving concurrent
sessions", and an "except on a pod serving one session" carve-out, are false twice: a §7.3 re-attach
reclaim onto a one-session replacement pod IS inside the staged §7.1 scope and does drive
`receiving_uploads → slot_cleanup` there, and the adapter's failed-start cleanup runs on either pod
class. The correct split is by WHICH BIND PATH the §7.1 obligation reaches, and the only uncovered
case is a pre-attached bind on a one-session pod, where `Binder.Prepare`'s reclaim is `failPhase`. A
reviewer's suggested wording invites the wrong split.
- **Leave the matched strings byte-identical when rewording the fence annotation.** The arrow string
`receiving_uploads ──→ slot_cleanup`, the two backticked cell values in DOCS-1's table row and
CODE-3's `{ReceivingUploads, SlotCleanup}` entry are each matched by a staged assertion; the tier-11
gate reads neither the parenthetical annotation nor the row's third column, which is why only those
two were reworded.
- **Do NOT write that the published error catalog "gains rows" for the two new codes.** That is the
pre-revision wording and it is wrong in both directions: DOCS-3 adds no row, and the two adapter
`ErrorCode` values are gateway-to-adapter codes that never reach the client-facing page. The
checklist's S7 line still carries the wrong form, which is why the standing Deferred names it.
- **`.spec-changes.md` has no trailing newline.** A scripted replacement whose search string ends in
`\n` fails on the last paragraph of that file.
- **review-log.md:2260 and :2268 record a DECISION to delete DOCS-3 and a WATCHOUT not to touch
`docs/reference/error-catalog.md:129`. Both are RETIRED** by the 2026-09-16 hand revision and the
round-5 §15.1 widening. A later agent reading either cold will undo DOCS-3; re-deleting it loops the
proposal.
- **MISTAKE nearly filed, and it cost a third of a round: SPEC-5's §6.2 `**Client visibility:**`
replacement and its "any other gRPC code".** It reads as though "any other" has no antecedent, so
the falsified quantification looks like it survives the re-key. It does not: the clause's first half
names the deterministic non-zero setup-command exit surfacing as `SETUP_COMMAND_FAILED`, and §15.1's
own row states that cause's code, so "any other" resolves to "other than `FailedPrecondition`" and
yields the intended meaning. Do not re-file it.
- **Do NOT file "§7.1's carve-out reads as a universal that excludes a §7.3 re-attach onto a
one-session pod".** `spec.2.review-feasibility.1` adjudicated that the implication runs one way only
(outside the obligation → one-session pod, never the converse), so a re-attach onto an exclusive
replacement pod is inside the obligation and does send a reclaim. Frame any exclusive-pod finding on
the §15.1/start arm, which IS explicitly concurrency-scoped.
- **Declined, so nobody re-derives it: the edge-case bullet's "refused on the reclaim hold while the
cleanup runs and on the attempt token afterwards" states disjoint sub-cases rather than two phases of
one.** When the adapter executed the cleanup and only the response was lost, the entry is gone
afterwards and the retry SUCCEEDS; when the adapter never received the reclaim, no hold exists and
only the token refuses; when the cleanup itself failed, staged §5.2 holds the identifier until the
pod terminates, so there is no "afterwards" at all. It over-claims pessimism only and lands in no
applied spec text.
- **Declined: the reclaim is sent once, with no durable record and no re-drive,** so a gateway crash,
a preStop drain or a rolling update between abandoning an attempt and sending its compensation
leaves a tokened entry no live attempt can adopt. It is stated as an accepted failure mode with two
named out-of-scope recoveries, and the residue bullets are barred from reopening. What is still owed
to a human is the standing Open on a rolling update versus a crash.
- **Do NOT file the §15.4 "`Shutdown` is not held" carve-out against §5.2's and §4.7.1's wide hold
predicate, now for the seventh time.** They reconcile on the vacuous-resolve reading: during the
hold the entry is already deregistered, so a `Shutdown` resolves nothing and the outcome is `absent`
either way. If a later round wants it closed, the fix is one clause in the §5.2 hold paragraph
naming the `Shutdown` exemption, never a §15.4 edit.
- **`spec/07:23`'s atomicity paragraph does not close with "The client never receives a
`session_id`..."** — it closes "...regardless of the flag." — although `spec-changes.md:38-40` says
it does. The insertion instruction at :444-447 quotes the real ending, so the edit applies cleanly.
Below the bar; do not re-derive.
- **A format pass may not mint a recommendation, alternatives-and-why-each-lost, or a confidence for
open decision 20.** It carries its question and its ground only, and it is now the section's sole
entry. The next adjudication firing owes the other three.
- **The 0078 overlap in `shutdown_drain_gate_race_test.go` is a merge hazard, not a design
conflict.** The two proposals' edit regions inside that file are distinct: 0078 touches
`socketDrainPod`'s accept timeout and the sequenced leg's assertion, while this proposal sets a
request field on four `ShutdownRequest` literals. The corrected impacts row says so in those terms,
so a later reader does not read it as a design conflict.
- **MISTAKE: a recorded WATCHOUT on a normative predicate sat unfiled for six rounds and shipped into five paraphrases.** The `mid_session` zero-value hazard was written down as a WATCHOUT (a later frame whose `mid_session` is false where the first was true IS a refusal) and no round filed it, so the symmetric per-frame predicate refused every ordinary multi-chunk §7.4 mid-session upload for six rounds of staging. Its "CODE-8 is the sender" framing is what kept it out of the spec lane, although the spec half was filable on its own. A recorded WATCHOUT on a normative predicate is filed or refuted, never carried.
- **The `PrepareWorkspace` per-frame predicate lives at SEVEN sites and they must stay one statement.** Two are normative (the §4.7.1 `PrepareWorkspace is client-streaming` paragraph and the §15.4 clause beginning "An adapter does not conform when it resolves the slot identifier more than once"); five are paraphrases in `non-spec-changes.md` (CODE-6's staging.go description :1307, CONF-1 clause (i) :1554, CONF-1's negative-case walkthrough :1609, the gateway tier-1 case :1966, the tier-3 clause list :2276). A fix touching only the normative pair leaves a conformance battery encoding the withdrawn symmetric rule.
- **The zero-value hazard has a SECOND face: the well-formedness pair.** If "non-mid-session with an empty `bind_attempt` is `INVALID_ARGUMENT`" is applied per frame, every later frame of a mid-session upload (empty token, default-false marker) is ill-formed and the upload is refused by well-formedness before the disagreement rule is reached. The fix has to say in the same paragraph that the admission rules are decided on the resolving frame.
- **Do NOT touch the Recycle-edges group's `a failed session` clause at spec/06:114-118.** It is correct for its own group, whose preamble requires the claim to be patched `bound → recycling` at the recycle boundary first, and deleting it breaks the clean-recycle retirement path. The defect is the OTHER `claimed ──→ draining` entry, the one under "Occupancy projection".
- **spec/06:80 carries TWO claim-deletion clauses and only one is corrected.** "a claim deleted on a pod with `recycle.enabled: false`" is the over-narrow one. "a claim deleted on a recycling pod under its limits projects `idle`" describes the `reserved → idle` edge after the claim has been patched, matches `case state.Reserved` in occupancy.go, and is correct as written.
- **The tempting wrong fix for the recycle qualifier is an exception clause** reading "and on a recycle-enabled pool the pre-attached failure is treated as a failed session". No actor writes that disposition on the pre-attached path, so the clause is a fiction the tree does not satisfy. It is exactly the hair the loop exists to prevent.
- **MISTAKE: the round-7 rewrite replaced an unconditional statement with a two-branch one.** The settled entry stated the retirement unconditionally; `git diff c1ab102f3..096b39656` on spec-changes.md replaced it with a two-branch form that reads as a tightening of the citation and was in fact the introduction of a claim no actor produces. Cost: one deep finding and a whole design round. The staged text drifted from its own settled record; check a "tightening" against the standing entry it narrows.
- **The one-session carve-out is a SWEEP, not a single edit.** Three rounds have now each found one more un-narrowed universal left behind by it: the §5.2 append, the accepted-failure bullet, and §4.7.9 step 5's trailing "rather than leaving the pod holding the session's state". Before declaring the sweep done, grep spec-changes.md for every sentence asserting that a pre-`running` bind failure is reclaimed, cleaned up, or leaves no state on the pod, and check each against the carve-out at spec-changes.md:641,:704.
- **The carve-out is scoped by STAGE, not by pod class, and that is what rescues it from the standing "do not split by pod class" trap.** It covers only a bind that fails at the workspace-preparation, setup or credential-assignment stage. `Binder.Resume` issues NO `PrepareWorkspace`, `FinalizeWorkspace`, `RunSetup` or `AssignCredentials` — it calls `connect`, `reserveResumeSlot` (kube and Redis only) and then `cl.Resume` — so no §7.3 re-attach can fail at one of those three stages and the carve-out and the re-attach arm do not overlap. Two round-8 lenses each nearly filed the pod-class objection. Do not re-file it without first showing a re-attach RPC at one of those stages. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/binder.go:1590-1629.
- **MISTAKE: a fix round corrected the `superseded` gloss at the §16.1 row only,** leaving the same too-narrow gloss standing at the §4.7 row, §4.7.1's outcome paragraph and §15.4's republication, even though the round that filed it wrote "if a fixer touches one, it must touch all three". Filed again at round 8. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:400,:741,:793 (narrow) versus :463,:894 (wide).
- **Do NOT "fix" spec-changes.md:400 by making a named reclaim REMOVE an untokened entry.** An untokened entry may be a live successor's own `StartSession` entry. The fix is the outcome NAME only: widen the gloss to cover an entry no bind attempt owns, leaving "performs neither, removes nothing" exactly as staged.
- **UNVERIFIED (two round-8 shards disagree and neither corrects the other):** `spec.8.review-client-surface.2` records the `superseded` gloss drift as twice-refuted and says do not file it a third time, while `spec.8.review-mechanism.2` filed it as two substantiated findings on the ground that :400's defect is an enumeration HOLE and :741/:793's is a false rationale clause. The newer mechanism reading is kept; whoever settles it should start from whether the two shards are describing the same defect.
- **The snapshot trap fired again, in its twelfth recorded form.** `diff -rq spec-r8 <live>` reports only the review log differing, while the real r7→r8 delta is 189 lines against `spec-r7`. Compounding it, `spec-r9` exists and is dated 2026-09-10, so it is PRE-revision epoch content. Take the newest snapshot that DIFFERS, by mtime, and never the highest-numbered one.
- **MISTAKE: a resumed round re-executes lenses whose shard is already on disk.** The round-8 shard directory already held a file for every lens, and several lenses re-ran a pass whose honest answer was already recorded. Read your own shard first and diff the staging's mtime against the shard's before spending the round; if the staging has not moved, say so and re-confirm rather than re-derive. A resumed run must still show its own evidence, so re-run the two or three mechanical checks that carry the verdict.
- **"Admission" throughout the staged §4.7.1 and §15.4 blocks means the ADAPTER's own gRPC request admission, never a Kubernetes admission webhook.** Third recording of this trap.
- **`onHoldTimeout` deregisters EVERY started session in pass 1 and runs the cleanups sequentially in pass 2,** so under the reclaim hold ("from the critical section that deregisters the entry until the cleanup finishes") the hold on member N is open for the whole of members 1..N-1. At the top `maxConcurrentSessions` that is a long hold. It is NOT a regression, because every one of those sessions is being terminated anyway. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:189-205.
- **The remedy for the two accepted-residue findings is ONE SENTENCE EACH in the staged SPEC-5 §4.7.1 block,** never a request to build remediation position 2 (a durable pending-reclaim record, a reaper, a tree generation, or a bind-scoped lock). A fixer who reads the finding as "close the residue" changes the design the loop settled. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:284-289,:309-314.
- **MISTAKE: a fix stage declared a lane converged without sweeping `### Open` for entries marked FILED.** Round 7 ran two fix groups plus a corrections pass and touched neither of the two accepted-residue findings, which the standing `### Open` still recorded as FILED with an unapplied remedy. Sweeping `### Open` for FILED-and-unapplied against the staging took ten minutes at round 8 and found both live. Do that sweep before declaring convergence.
- **The two new §16.1 rows for the GATEWAY counters have NO gate at all.** `adapter_metric_catalog_test.go` reads only `pkg/adapter/metrics.go`, and nothing reconciles the gateway catalog against §16.1 or metrics.md, so if CODE-9's metrics.md rows are dropped, tier 11 stays green.
- **DOCS-2 claims `docs/reference/adapter-contract.md` "publishes `ConfigureWorkspace` as idempotent". The page does not.** Its row reads only "Point a pre-connected session at the finalized `cwd` (SDK-warm mode). Timeout: 10s."; the idempotency claim lives at spec/04:673 alone. DOCS-2 must either add the idempotency clause to that row or drop the self-reference. A separate trap bars filing the neighbouring `DemoteSDK` row; this is a different row and a different defect. EVIDENCE: docs/reference/adapter-contract.md:63; non-spec-changes.md:1826,:1828.
- **`ConfigureWorkspace` DOES create a registry entry** (`claimSessionSlot` at sdkwarm.go:216), so §5.2's entry-creating request list is complete and correct. A lens that assumes `ConfigureWorkspace` only "points" an existing session at a cwd will wrongly file the list as over-broad.
- **CORRECTS the orchestrator brief: `ShutdownResponse` does not gain `slot_was_started`, and `slot_reclaim` is new rather than kept.** The brief for this run states "ShutdownResponse keeps slot_reclaim and gains slot_was_started (4)"; both halves are wrong against the staging. A lens checking the §15.4 wire enumeration against the brief will file a phantom omission. Three round-8 lenses each spent time on it.

One round-8 lens read the pointer as scoped to the action list and declined; the standing dead-end entry explicitly leaves a re-open argued from the bullet's own trigger open. - **`sessionPolicy` "Pod reuse" (`maxConcurrentSessions: 1` with `recycle.enabled: true`) is a REAL configuration,** so the recycling half of the one-session carve-out is not vacuous. A reviewer who assumes one-session pods never recycle will wrongly refute any finding that rests on it. EVIDENCE: spec/05:425.
- **Candidate DROPPED, do not re-file: a surviving residue entry on a reused single-session pod suppresses the next session's MCP arming.** `claimPodMCPStartLocked` arms the intra-pod MCP surface only when `len(s.slots) != 1` is false, and `slotCount()` is the quantity §28.5.3's resolve-or-reject rule reads, so one residue entry makes every unaddressed frame unresolvable for the life of the pod. The behaviour is real, shipped and unchanged by any staged deliverable, and the staged sentence asserts nothing false, so the remedy would be new spec text about a pre-existing residue class. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slotsession.go:109-115,:401-407; pkg/adapter/podscrub.go:39-81.
- **Candidate DROPPED, do not re-file: the crash-stranded tokened entry is a failure mode less reliable than shipped.** Today a retry ADOPTS the stale entry (`ensureSlotStateLocked` returns the existing `*slotState`) and succeeds; under the staging every later attempt at that session on that pod is refused `SLOT_BIND_ATTEMPT_SUPERSEDED` until the pod is replaced. The mechanism is real and the adoption half is verifiable, but the proposal states it bullet for bullet as an accepted failure mode, its incidence is crash-only, and the accounting drains the pod at `maxConcurrentSessions: 2` on one refusal. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/slot.go:104-107; spec-changes.md:296-307.
- **Candidate DROPPED, and it cost the most time of any round-8 candidate: "SPEC-4's retiring exit is infeasible on a sequential-reuse pool."** The spec sentence it rests on is real (spec/06:80) and `failPhase`'s drain really is a bare `DeleteClaim`, but the shipped projection resolves a claim DELETE on a `claimed` pod to `Draining` unconditionally of recycle, and spec/05:455 plus §6.2's pod-crash paragraph both retire a failed session's pod regardless of recycle settings. Two rounds have now walked this to the end.

### Open

- **CODE-1 comment phrasing** — UNVERIFIED: non-spec-changes.md:72-77 still carries "the runtime teardown runs for a session the pod's shared runtime process has been given" for a gate that reads `st.started`; confirm G2 landed the rewrite. See `spec.1.fix-G1.1`.
- **Tier-4 fixture extension** — OPEN: the staged tier-4 datastore-crossing case needs a second placeable Sandbox, a `MaxConcurrentSessions > 1` pool and a wired session server, none of which `recycle_scrub_path_test.go` has, and extending `recycleCluster` changes a fixture seven cases share. Decide between extending it and splitting the retry-re-binds half down to tier 1. See `non-spec-recheck.1.review-applicability.1` and `non-spec-recheck.1.review-feasibility.1`.
- **Socket runtime on a recycling pool** — OPEN: after any last `Runtime.Close` the listener is gone, so the next session's `Runtime.Start` accepts on a closed listener. Either socket runtimes never land on recycling pools or the recycle path is broken for them. Pre-existing; worth its own finding. See `spec.1.fix-design-G2.1`.
- **§6.2 fence versus §6.2 prose on `leaked` at concurrency 1** — OPEN (filed as a separate finding, not for this proposal): the fence scopes `slot_cleanup ──→ leaked` to concurrent occupancy while §6.2's own leaked-semantics paragraph, §5.2's `ceil(maxConcurrentSessions/2)` threshold at 1, and `slothealth.Tracker` all apply `leaked` at concurrency 1. See `spec.1.fix-design-G2.1`.
- **`terminate` frame reason value** — OPEN, re-scoped: under the design as it now stands a bound-but-unstarted reclaim runs no runtime teardown, no close and no frame, because §4.7 puts the §15.4.2 signal inside the teardown and CODE-1 moves the drain inside the `started` block. The `reason`-enum question survives only for a reclaim of a session whose start WAS admitted, where `drainReason` maps `"slot_bind_failed"` to `session_complete`. The wire stays valid; nobody has decided whether that is the intended value. Re-scope the item before spending a round on the enum. See `spec.7.review-operational.1`.
- **Mid-start `Shutdown` bricks the pod silently** — UNVERIFIED, and narrower than it reads: `Close(sessionID)` for a session not in `p.active` still takes the last-close teardown whenever `p.active` is empty and `p.connected` is true, closing the listener bound once at adapter start. Reaching `connected && active empty` needs the `Interrupt` path, which closes the conn without clearing `connected`. Pre-existing, reachable only after an Interrupt of the last active session, and the remedy is code-lane. See `spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1` and `spec.2.review-reliability.2`.
- **"Does not acknowledge" versus an unclean answer** — UNVERIFIED: the staged §7.1 predicate is "a reclaim the adapter does not acknowledge", while the gateway's shipped predicate is `err != nil || !cleanly` and CODE-1 deliberately makes `exited_cleanly` false for a not-started reclaim whose tree removal failed. The §7.1 sentence may want "does not acknowledge, or answers that the release did not complete". See `spec.1.review-feasibility.1` and `spec.1.review-kubernetes.1`.
- **§4.1's retired vocabulary** — OPEN: SPEC-1's §4.1 replacement retires "per-session teardown" while the retained second sentence still says "The per-slot teardown and the whole-pod teardown are the same operation on the same address". After the split there are three named operations and the retained sentence names two the new sentence does not define. See `spec.1.review-fresh.1`.
- **`receiving_uploads` on an upload-free plan** — UNVERIFIED: if the §6.2 edge label "workspace materialization begins for this slot" is read as `PrepareWorkspace` rather than as entering the staging stage, a reclaim on the upload-free branch has no legal edge out of `slot_assigned` and SPEC-4 does not cover it. The charitable reading was assumed. UNVERIFIED and disputed: `spec-recheck.3.review-reliability.1` reads SPEC-4's own prose ("Every earlier stage of the §4.7.9 step-5 bind sequence ... leave the slot in `receiving_uploads`") as asserting the charitable reading outright and therefore closing the item, while the later `f2.open-decisions.out-of-scope-defects.slot-assigned-terminal` records it as still unsettled and rests its scope call on the charitable reading. Neither corrects the other. See `spec.1.review-kubernetes.1`.
- **§5.2's bullet at concurrency 1** — UNVERIFIED: whether §5.2's `**Slot failure and cleanup**` bullet is meant to govern the slot release on a `maxConcurrentSessions: 1` pod at all, given the staged §4.7 row defines the slot release as "the §5.2 slot cleanup" with no concurrency scoping. `spec.1.review-feasibility.1` argues it does not over-reach; `spec.1.review-performance.1` still wants it decided.
- **spec/05 versus spec/06 on failed-session pod retirement** — OPEN as a pre-existing wording tension, CLOSED for this proposal's sentence: spec/05:455 and :492 say a session that ends in failure or a crash always retires its pod, while spec/06:283 says the pod "is marked `failed` and released back to the pool (or terminated if unhealthy)". The §6.2 fence resolves it in favour of retirement, which is what makes the staged §7.1 citation hold. Predates this proposal, and a fix elsewhere should not deepen it. See `spec.1.review-security.1` and `spec.2.review-operational.1`.
- **`terminate` frame `deadlineMs`** — UNVERIFIED, pre-existing and code-lane, now NARROWED: the frame requires `deadlineMs` with `minimum: 100`, `RuntimeOps.Terminate` serialises it `omitempty`, and the two shipped gateway callers (`slotbinder.go:542`, `binder.go:2043`) still pass 0, so a Full-level runtime receives a frame missing a required field at an ordinary session end. The `budget/2` split answers it FOR THE COMPENSATION'S CALL SITE ONLY, which becomes the one `Shutdown` in the tree that satisfies the minimum; the item stands everywhere else. Whoever owns the tier-3 runtime-ops contract test should confirm. See `spec.2.review-client-surface.1` and `non-spec-recheck-2.2.fix-design-G1.1`.
- **`/sessions/` and `/artifacts/` in the widened action list** — UNVERIFIED: `RemoveTree` removes four trees and SPEC-3's widened §5.2 list names two of them plus the timers. The omission predates the proposal, so it was judged incompleteness rather than a defect, but a completeness lens may disagree. See `spec.2.review-citations.1`.
- **"incomplete enumeration" under §29's preamble rule** — OPEN: §29 item 12 gains no fourth `Shutdown` trigger from SPEC-2, and the preamble subordinates a trace only where it DISAGREES. Somebody should decide once whether an incomplete enumeration counts as disagreement and record the answer, because the question returns every round. See `spec.2.review-citations.1`.
- **Concurrent resume leaves a freshly claimed pod at occupancy 1** — OPEN, and the earlier narrowing was CORRECTED: the hold is bounded by §4.6.1 orphan GC at `claimOrphanTimeout`, not by the §5.2 replacement trigger. At `maxConcurrentSessions >= 3` the trigger never fires, because `UnhealthyThreshold` is `(maxConcurrent+1)/2`, `slothealth.Tracker` counts per pod, and each §7.3 retry claims a DIFFERENT fresh idle pod, so no replacement pod accumulates two leaks. `PodHasActiveSession` is false on a freshly claimed replacement pod (`bumpRecoveryGeneration` runs only on success, so `row.PodAssignment` still names the OLD pod), which is what makes predicate 1 fire. The proposal itself is not wrong here; its churn bullet scopes the drain claim to `maxConcurrentSessions: 2`. Whether `ClaimSlot` pass 1 treats such a pod as slot-bearing is still unverified. See `spec.2.review-performance.1` and `non-spec.2.review-kubernetes.1`.

- **Does §7.3 owe a release step at all?** — UNVERIFIED: the §7.3 appended sentence orders the reclaim "before the replacement pod is released", but §7.3's numbered flow states no replacement-pod release on any branch, and §6.2's three non-terminal `resuming` failure bullets state no disposition for the half-claimed pod either. - **Do the other three `resuming` failure bullets need the reclaim clause?** — UNVERIFIED: pod crash, the 300s watchdog and non-retryable errors state no pod release today, so there is nothing for the clause to attach to, but §6.2 calls itself the authoritative enumeration of every edge out of `resuming`. The gauge half is a recorded dead end; whether the Redis-equivalence half is a separate spec finding is undecided and is not 0081's. See `spec.5.review-performance.2`.
- **Should the timer cancellation be conditioned on the credential removal?** — UNVERIFIED: `deregisterSlotLocked` cancels the §4.9 direct-mode timers at the top of `Shutdown` while `removeSlotTree` runs after the drain signal and `Runtime.Close`, so a cleanup whose credential removal fails leaves a direct-mode key with its enforcement point disarmed.
